# Changes

## 11.25.0.0 06/11/2026

* `ITransactionCommon.TransactionTypeName` and `BaseLedgerEntry.LedgerEntryTypeName`, `NodeBase.LedgerEntryTypeName` hold the name of the type: for a type the SDK does not know, where the enum is `Unknown`, the name the node sent, otherwise the enum member's name. `TransactionTypeName` is `Unknown` when the JSON had no type; `BaseLedgerEntry.LedgerEntryTypeName` is `null`, as in `FinalFields` and `PreviousFields`. Assigning the enum replaces the name. `ToJson()` and the node converters write the name, so `TransactionType` and `LedgerEntryType` no longer turn into the word `Unknown` on the way back to JSON. These properties now carry the JSON field through `TransactionTypeNameConverter` and `LedgerEntryTypeNameConverter`; the enum properties are `[JsonIgnore]` and keep their meaning. Tests: `TestUUnknownTypeName` in `Xrpl.Tests`
* `BaseLedgerEntry.Sponsor` reads `sfSponsor`, the common ledger field naming the sponsor of an entry's reserve (XLS-68); it was left in `UnknownFields`. Tests: `TestUFeePayer.LedgerEntry_*`, `TestUFeePayer.MetadataNode_ReadsAChangedSponsorFromPreviousFields`, `TestISponsoredTypes.Sponsored_Payment_SponsorCreatedAccount` in `Xrpl.Tests`
* `FeePayerExtensions` (`Xrpl.Models.Transactions`): `tx.GetFeePayer()` returns the account a transaction's fee is charged to, as rippled `STTx::getFeePayerID`: the `Sponsor` when `SponsorFlags` has `spfSponsorFee`, otherwise the `Delegate`, otherwise the `Account`. `tx.GetFeePayer(meta)` returns `null` when the metadata shows the fee came out of a prepaid `Sponsorship` object (`FeePayerType::SponsorPreFunded`). With a `Fee` of zero, as on a Batch inner transaction, nothing is charged and the result is who would have paid. Tests: `TestUFeePayer`, `TestISponsorship.FeePayer_*` in `Xrpl.Tests`
* **Breaking:** `NodeConverterBase.ReadLedgerEntryType` is removed. Migration: read `LedgerEntryType` from the node
* **Behaviour change:** a `TrustSet` read from JSON has only the flags the JSON carries: `TrustSet` implements `IJsonOnDeserializing` and clears the constructor's `tfSetNoRipple` before the members are read, so with no `Flags` it reads as `null` (was `tfSetNoRipple`). This covers `TransactionRequestConverter`, `Deserialize<TrustSet>` and `SignatureResult.GetTx`. `new TrustSet()` still presets `tfSetNoRipple`. What breaks: code that parses a JSON template without `Flags` and submits it no longer sends `tfSetNoRipple`. Migration: put the flag in the JSON or assign `Flags` after parsing. Tests: `TestUTrustSetFlags` in `Xrpl.Tests`
* `LedgerBinaryConverter`, `TransactionRequestConverter` and `TransactionResponseConverter` find the member that picks the type through a copy of the reader (`JsonMemberReader`, internal) and deserialize from the reader they were given, instead of a `JsonDocument`, `GetRawText` and a second parse. `LOConverter.GetBaseRippleLO` reads the `JsonElement` straight into the model. Tests: `TestUJsonMemberReader`
* **Behaviour change:** a `tx_json` or `ledger` value that is not an object fails with `JsonException` from the model, not `InvalidOperationException` from `TryGetProperty`. The `JsonException` inside `Cannot read X` carries positions in the outer document, not the nested string
* `B58.DivMod256` and `DivMod58` take `byte[]`; `HashUtils.DoubleDigest` uses one disposed `SHA256` for both rounds, and `HashUtils.Sha256` is gone. `Xrpl.AddressCodec` is 11.25.0.0. Tests: `TestUInnerTxId`
* `SubmitSugar` looks up the sponsorship that decides whether a sponsor must sign by the transaction's initiator, the `Delegate` when there is one, as rippled does; it used the `Account`, and it reads every page of `account_objects`, not the first. Tests: `TestUSponsorSignatureRequired`
* The loan a payment quote leaves (`LoanAfter`) keeps `Sponsor`. Tests: `TestULoanPaymentProjection`

## 11.24.0.0 03/11/2026

* `DateTimeUtils.ToRippleTime(DateTimeOffset)` and `ToRippleTime(DateTime)` return the ledger (Ripple-epoch) time of an instant as `uint`, rounded down to the whole second and saturating: a time before 2000-01-01 is `0`, one past `uint.MaxValue` seconds (year 2136) is `uint.MaxValue`; a `Local` `DateTime` is converted to UTC first and an `Unspecified` one is taken as UTC. `FromRippleTime(long)` is the inverse, `RippleEpoch` (`DateTimeOffset`) and `RippleEpochUnixSeconds` are public. `UnixTimeToRippleTime` is unchanged (it rounds to the nearest second, ties to even, where xrpl.js rounds ties up; 0.7 s rounds up); use `ToRippleTime` for an expiration or other deadline. Tests: `TestUTimeConversionTests.TestUToRippleTime*`, `TestUFromRippleTimeRoundTrips`, `TestURippleEpochIsTheFirstOf2000`, `TestUTimeConversionLocalZone.TestUToRippleTimeConvertsLocalToUtc` in `Xrpl.Tests`
* **Behaviour change:** the SDK's internal copies of the ledger-time conversion now use `ToRippleTime`, which changes the edges: `RippleDateTimeConverter.Write` writes `0` for a date before 2000 (was a negative number) and `uint.MaxValue` for one past 2136 (was a larger number the UInt32 field cannot hold); the internal `LendingMath.RippleSeconds`, behind the loan quotes (`LoanPayments`, `LoanManagement`), `AmmPool.FromAmmInfo` and the `DexSnapshotLoader`/`OfferCreateCrossing` expirations, returns `0` for a time before 2000 (was negative, and `(uint)` of it wrapped to a huge value) and `uint.MaxValue` past 2136 (was larger, and `(uint)` of it wrapped to a small one). Two times that are both before 2000 therefore compare equal: in `AmmPool.FromAmmInfo`, an auction slot that expires in 1995 read at a close time in 1990 used to count as active (discounted fee 50) and now counts as expired (the pool fee, 500). Ledger times are never negative or past 2136, so this only shows for hand-built input. Tests: `TestURippleDateTimeConverter.Serialize_DateTimeBeforeTheEpoch_ReturnsZero`, `Serialize_DateTimeMaxValue_SaturatesToUInt32Max`, `TestUAmmLiquidity.FromAmmInfo_SlotAndCloseTimeBeforeTheEpoch_BothCountAsZeroSeconds` in `Xrpl.Tests`
* `AmmMath.Sqrt(decimal)` is public: the decimal square root (Newton's method, `ArgumentOutOfRangeException` for a negative value) that the pool formulas use, for consumers that compute on top of them. `AmmMath.LPTokensForSingleAssetDeposit` multiplies first as before and divides `(r - c) / (1 + c)` before multiplying by the LP token balance only when the product would overflow (rippled evaluates equation 3 in `Number`); a huge deposit into a small pool, such as 1e22 against a balance of 1 with 1e10 LP tokens, threw `OverflowException`. Results for every input that did not overflow are identical to 11.23.0.0: dividing first everywhere was measured to lose significant digits on small deposits (relative difference up to about 5e-16 on the measured grid: 5e-16 at a deposit ratio of 1e-15, 1.3e-17 at 1e-12, 6e-21 at 1e-9, with an LP balance of 1e6 and a 1% fee), so the order is chosen per magnitude. Tests: `TestUAmmMath.TestUAHugeDepositIntoATinyPoolDoesNotOverflow`, `TestUSmallDepositsKeepTheDigitsOfTheMultiplyFirstOrder`, `TestUSqrtSettlesAcrossTheDecimalRange`, `TestUAnUnbalancedTwoSidedDepositIsCreditedAtTheSmallerRatio` in `Xrpl.Tests`
* `AssetIdentity` (`Xrpl.Models.Common`) holds the asset-identity rules that code pricing, routing or matching assets needs: `IsHexCurrencyCode` (exactly 40 hex digits, either case; accepts `null`), `CurrencyEquals` (two 40-digit hex codes compare case-insensitively, everything else ordinally, so `XPM` and `xpm` are different tokens), `IssuerEquals` (ordinal), `AssetEquals` (code plus issuer, a null, empty or whitespace issuer counts as no issuer) and `IsXrp(code)` / `IsXrp(code, issuer)` (exactly `XRP`, ordinal; the two-argument form also requires an empty issuer). `Hashes.IsHexCurrencyCode` now delegates to it: same result for every string, `ArgumentNullException` for `null` as before, and no regular expression per call.
* **Behaviour change:** `Currency.IsXrp()` compares the code case-sensitively. It upper-cased the code, so a `Currency` with the code `xrp` or `Xrp` and no issuer was reported as native XRP; the exact code `XRP` is reserved for native XRP, so an issued currency cannot use it (the JSON code `XRP` is parsed as native, and the hex form of ASCII `XRP` gets `temBAD_CURRENCY`), while `xrp` is a legal issued-currency code. A legal IOU always has an issuer and `IsXrp()` already required `Issuer == null`, so it was never reported as XRP: only malformed input (such a code without an issuer) is affected. What changes for callers: `new Currency { CurrencyCode = "xrp" }.IsXrp()` and the same for `"Xrp"` return `false` (were `true`); `"XRP"` without an issuer is still `true`, `"XRP"` with an issuer is still `false`. The only caller on a `Currency` inside the SDK is `XrplAmount.AssetOf` (so `FromCurrency`): for such input the asset is now `IssuedCurrency { Currency = "xrp", Issuer = null }` and keeps the code `xrp` instead of being normalized to `XRP`; `XrplAmount.KindOf` goes through `IssuedCurrency.IsXrp()`, which looks only at the issuer and the MPT id, so the amount is still classified as `AmountKind.Xrp`. `XrplAmount.ToCurrency()` still writes `XRP` for that malformed input (unchanged from 11.23). Code that relied on `IsXrp()` matching lower-case input must upper-case the code first (`ToUpperInvariant()`) before calling it; `AssetIdentity.IsXrp` is case-sensitive as well. Tests: `TestUAssetIdentity`, `TestUCurrency.IsXrp_LowercaseXrpWithoutIssuer_ReturnsFalse`, `IsXrp_MixedCaseXrp_ReturnsFalse`, `FromCurrency_LowercaseXrpWithoutIssuer_KeepsTheCodeAndIsStillXrpKind` in `Xrpl.Tests`
* `LedgerAmountGrid` (`Xrpl.Amounts`) rounds an amount onto what the ledger can hold, in decimal arithmetic. `RoundDown(value, isXrp)` goes toward zero (the form for a figure to be received), `RoundUp` away from zero (a figure that bounds a spend, such as `SendMax`); the grid is symmetric around zero (`RoundUp(-0.1234567m, true)` is `-0.123457m`, `RoundDown` is `-0.123456m`). XRP has six decimal places; an issued amount keeps a mantissa in `[1e15, 1e16)`, that is `15 - msd` decimal places for the most significant digit at position `msd`, at most 28, and from 1e16 the step grows past one (`RoundDown(12345678901234567m, false)` is `12345678901234560`, `RoundUp` is `12345678901234570`). The scaled value is first rounded to six places to drop the residue of additions (a quantity of `400000` plus `1e-16` stays `400000` when rounded up, instead of becoming `400000.000001`), then truncated or taken to the next whole step. Unlike a plain truncation or ceiling this can land on the other side of the input by up to 5e-7 of a step (banker's rounding at the midpoint): `RoundDown(1.23456799999999m, true)` is `1.234568`, `RoundDown(1m - 1e-23m, false)` is `1`, while `RoundDown(1.2345679m, true)` is `1.234567` and `RoundDown(1m - 1e-17m, false)` is `0.9999999999999999`. `Step(value, isXrp)` is the distance between neighbouring grid values at that magnitude (`1e-6` for XRP, `1e-15` at 1, `10` at 12345678901234567, `1e-28` at the smallest `decimal`), whatever the sign; **`Step(0, ...)` throws `ArgumentOutOfRangeException`** for XRP and issued alike, because zero has no most significant digit and therefore no step (a scan for one never ends). `IsBelowSmallest(value, isXrp)` is true for an XRP amount with `0 < value < SmallestXrp` (`1e-6`); issued amounts never are. `XrpDecimals` (6) and `IssuedSignificantDigits` (16) are public. Above about 7.9e22 an XRP amount already has at most five fractional digits and is returned unchanged rather than overflowing the scaling; `RoundUp` of an issued amount within one step of `decimal.MaxValue` or `decimal.MinValue` throws `OverflowException` (the sign is applied after the magnitude is rounded), a negative XRP amount that large is returned unchanged, while `RoundDown(decimal.MaxValue, false)` is `79228162514264330000000000000`. Unlike a plain decimal-places grid, the step is 10 from 1e16 (a value of `12345678901234567` is cut to `12345678901234560`, not passed through with seventeen digits), the residue is removed, and `Step` refuses zero. MPT amounts (whole numbers up to 2^63-1) are not on this grid and are not covered: use `Math.Truncate` / `Math.Ceiling` for them. `Currency.ValueAsNumber`'s fallback at the very top of the `decimal` range now uses `LedgerAmountGrid.RoundDown` instead of its own `Math.Log10`-based truncation: same result there (the 16 leading digits, the rest zeros). Tests: `TestULedgerAmountGrid` in `Xrpl.Tests`
* `MemoRules.TryGetSerializedLength(object memos, out int length)` measures a `Memos` array the way `MemoRules.Validate` does (rippled `isMemoOkay`: the serialized array, without its own start and end markers, at most `MaxSerializedLength` = 1024 bytes), so a memo form and the signing check count the same thing. `memos` is a collection of `{ "Memo": { ... } }` elements or a `JsonArray`; the result is `false` with `length = 0` for `null`, a non-array, an empty array, an element that is not a `Memo` object, and content the codec cannot serialize (a value that is not hex). A single memo costs `n + 5` bytes for `n >= 193` bytes of `MemoData` and nothing else (1019 fills the limit), and `n + 17` when it also carries `MemoFormat` = `text/plain` (1007 fills it). `TryGetSerializedLength(IEnumerable<MemoWrapper> memos, out int length)` is the typed overload for `TransactionCommon.Memos` (the compiler rejects a string or a single `Memo`, which the `object` overload answers `false` for); both give the same length. Any content the codec fails on, while parsing or while writing, and an object `System.Text.Json` cannot convert to JSON (a `double.NaN` value, a getter that throws), is "nothing to measure" too, whatever the exception: a JSON `null` anywhere in a memo, a field of the wrong length or kind, an unknown enum name, an empty path in a `Paths` set, a field past the serializer's 918744-byte cap. The input is arbitrary and the codec's failures on it are not one family, so the method does not list them; it throws only critical runtime failures (`OutOfMemoryException` and the like). Nothing is lost by this: building the transaction runs the same codec and reports the cause in its own words. `Validate` now goes through it; its behaviour is unchanged. Tests: `TestUMemoRules.TestUOneMemoOfDataOnlyFillsTheLimitAt1019Bytes`, `TestUAPlainTextMemoFillsTheLimitAt1007Bytes`, `TestUAPlainTextMemoCostsItsDataPlus17Bytes`, `TestUSeveralMemosAreMeasuredAsOneArray`, `TestUAJsonArrayIsMeasuredLikeTheCollectionItCameFrom`, `TestUNothingToMeasureIsNotALength`, `TestUMembersTheCodecCannotReadAreNotMeasurable`, `TestUANullDeeperInAMemoIsNotMeasurable`, `TestUAMemoFieldPastTheSerializerCapIsNotMeasurable`, `TestUABadMptAmountInAMemoIsNotMeasurable`, `TestUAnEmptyPathInAMemoIsNotMeasurable`, `TestUContentTheCodecFailsOnIsNotMeasurable`, `TestUAnObjectTheSerializerCannotConvertIsNotMeasurable`, `TestUTypedMemosAreMeasuredLikeTheDictionaryShape`, `TestUTypedNothingToMeasureIsNotALength` in `Xrpl.Tests`

## 11.23.0.0 02/11/2026

* `Hashes.GetNFTokenPageOwner(pageIndex)` returns the classic address of the owner of an `NFTokenPage` from its ledger index: the high 160 bits are the owner's AccountID (rippled `keylet::nftpage_min`/`nftpage_max`; the low 96 bits come from an NFTokenID). Upper and lower case hex are accepted; a null, non-64-character or non-hex index raises `ArgumentException`. Tests: `TestUHashes.TestGetNFTokenPageOwner*` in `Xrpl.Tests`

## 11.22.0.0 28/10/2026

* **Breaking:** `XrplWallet.FromMnemonic` (BIP-39) stores the secp256k1 private key with the `00` prefix, as seed-derived keys and xrpl.js do (`0013FC…`, 66 hex characters; was the bare 64). `K256KeyPair.Sign` reads the key as unsigned (`new BigInteger(1, …)`): a raw 32-byte key whose first byte is `0x80` or above was read as negative and produced signatures that do not verify, which was the case for about half of all mnemonic wallets. `K256KeyPair.Pk` pads the key to 32 bytes. What breaks: code that compares or stores `FromMnemonic(...).PrivateKey` sees the prefixed form. Migration: none for signing; a stored 64-hex key still signs correctly
* **Breaking:** `RequestManager` completes request promises with `TaskCreationOptions.RunContinuationsAsynchronously` and removes a request before completing it, in `Resolve` and `Reject`. The caller's continuation ran inline on the socket receive thread while the id was still registered, so `GetBalances` could fail with `Response with id … is already pending` on its second page, and a slow continuation held up every later frame. What breaks: a continuation with `TaskContinuationOptions.ExecuteSynchronously` on a request task no longer runs on the thread that handles the response. `GetBalances` also clears `AccountLinesRequest.Id` between pages. A request's timeout timer and cancellation reject only that request and remove only its own entry and timer (`TaskInfo.TimeoutTimer`), so one that fires late cannot fail a new request that reuses the id
* `SubmitSugar.SubmitMulti` (both overloads) passes `failHard` to `submit`; it always sent `false`
* `AutofillSugar.CheckAccountDeleteBlockers` throws `XrplException` when the account has deletion blockers; it set the exception on a `TaskCompletionSource` nobody awaited and always returned normally
* `EscrowFinish(string owner, uint offerSequence, string condition, string fulfillment)` chains to the default constructor and sets `TransactionType`; it left it null
* `BookIndex.Observe` adds a book with an XRP side from a created `DirectoryNode`: metadata `NewFields` leave out the zero `TakerPaysCurrency`/`TakerPaysIssuer` (or `TakerGets*`) of the XRP side, and the directory was dropped. A directory with both sides absent or without `ExchangeRate` is still ignored; `FromLedgerAsync` keeps requiring both sides
* `CurrencyExtensions.NormalizeCurrencyCode` reads the text of an XLS-16 (`02`) code as bytes 8 to 20 before decoding; text with multi-byte UTF-8 characters threw `ArgumentOutOfRangeException`, which reached `IssuedCurrency.ToString` and `CurrencyReadableName` for any trust line with such a code
* `MptValue(string)` (the MPT branch of `Amount.FromJson`) refuses a fractional value and one above `long.MaxValue` with `BinaryCodecException`, as `NativeValue` does for drops; it truncated the fraction and wrapped the high bit into the type flags. The value is read exactly (`1e3` and `1.5e1` are whole numbers), not through `decimal`, which rounded a fraction past 28 digits to a whole number
* `Currency.ValueAsNumber` setter writes an MPT amount (`MPTokenIssuanceID` set) as a whole number, so `1e16` and `long.MaxValue` are exact instead of `G16` exponent notation, and throws `ArgumentException` for a fraction. **Breaking:** `Currency.ValueAsXrp` setter throws `ArgumentException` for a value with more than six decimal places; it wrote fractional drops. Migration: round the XRP value to drops before assigning. `Currency.IsNFT14D` returns false for a null `CurrencyCode`
* `ServerFeaturesRequest.Feature` serializes as `feature`; rippled reads the lowercase key and ignored `Feature`
* `Amount.DecimalValue` accepts a negative IOU value (`NumberStyles.AllowLeadingSign`)
* `Hashes.LedgerSpaceHex(string)` uses the enum value, as the enum overload does (`Account` is `0061`; it was `0041`), and throws `ArgumentException` for an unknown name instead of answering for the first enum member
* `Uint32.FromJson` reads a `JsonValue` built in code from `int`, `uint`, `long`, `ulong`, `short`, `ushort`, `byte`, `sbyte`, `double`, `float` and `decimal` with a range check, and a parsed `7.0` or `7e0`; a fractional value raises `OverflowException`. It threw `InvalidOperationException` for anything but `uint`. `BatchNormalizer.NormalizeInnerTransaction` reads a numeric `Flags` through `Uint32.FromJson`, so flags set from code are OR-ed with `tfInnerBatchTxn` instead of replaced by it, and a `Flags` that is not a UInt32 raises `ValidationException`
* `LedgerSequenceHelper.GetLastLedgerSequence`, `SubmitSugar.IsSigned` and `SubmitSugar.IsAccountDelete` read the fields of a typed or anonymous transaction from its JSON form; the fallback parsed the hex blob returned by `XrplBinaryCodec.Encode` as JSON and threw
* `Hash` overrides `Equals(object)` and `GetHashCode` consistently with `IEquatable<Hash>`; `Hash128`, `Hash160` and `Hash256` check the buffer length with `ArgumentException` instead of `Contract.Assert`, which is compiled out of release builds
* `GetOrderbook(ledgerIndex)` builds a numeric ledger index with `new LedgerIndex((uint)ledgerIndex)`; it cast the number to `LedgerIndexType`. `-1` stays `validated`
* `CrossChainPayment.CreateCrossChainPayment` copies the source payment member by member, so `SourceTag`, `TicketSequence`, `NetworkID` and every other field survive; only `Memos` and `TransactionSignature` change
* `Validation.ValidatePayment` accepts `Paths` of any collection shape, as `DictionaryObjectConverter` produces them (`List<object>` of `List<object>` of `Dictionary<string, object>`); `Validation.IsPathSet` is added. A null `Paths` is accepted and a wrong shape is invalid. It rejected every `Paths` that was not a `List<List<Dictionary<string, object>>>`
* `BinarySerializer.EncodeVl` caps a variable-length field at 918744 bytes, as rippled and xrpl.js do (was 918745)
* `SerializedEnumeration.ReadOrdinal` reads a multi-byte ordinal as `(a << 8) | b`; `(a >> 8) + b` returned the last byte for the two-byte `TransactionType` and `LedgerEntryType` ordinals
* `EcdsaSignature.DecodeFromDer` returns null for a malformed DER signature (truncated, not a sequence, not two integers, trailing bytes, not the strict DER encoding rippled requires) instead of throwing `IOException`, `ArgumentException` or `NullReferenceException`, so `K256KeyPair.Verify` answers false
* `DexSnapshotLoader` reads `DepositPreauth` objects page by page (`Limit` 400, `Marker`), as `PathfindingSource` does; it read the first page only
* `XrplClient.Connect` and `ChangeServer` pass their `CancellationToken` into the `server_info` request that reads the network id
* `UnixDateTimeConverter` converts a `DateTimeKind.Local` value to UTC before it writes the timestamp; `Unspecified` is taken as UTC (documented on the type)
* `Validation.ValidateDIDSet` counts an empty string as a present field, since it clears that field of the DID; it refuses the transaction when all three fields are absent or all three are present and empty (`temEMPTY_DID`)
* `Validation.ValidateNFTokenCreateOffer` and `Validation.ValidateNFTokenMint` compare `Account` with `Owner`, `Destination` and `Issuer` by value (`string.Equals`, ordinal); `==` on `object` compared references
* `BatchUtils.ToBatchTx` and `BatchUtils.Build` work on a member-wise copy of each transaction (a JSON copy would restore constructor defaults such as `TrustSet`'s `tfSetNoRipple` that the caller cleared); the caller's model was stripped of its signature and `LastLedgerSequence`, given `Fee` 0 and flagged `tfInnerBatchTxn`
* `MPTokenMetadataSchema.ToJson` writes non-ASCII as UTF-8 (`JavaScriptEncoder.UnsafeRelaxedJsonEscaping`), as `JSON.stringify` does; `GetByteSize` measures the same bytes. The default escaper wrote `\uXXXX`, which overstated the size
* `AutofillSugar.ValidateAccountAddress` compares an X-address tag with `Common.TryGetUInt32`; the `(int)` unboxing threw `InvalidCastException` for a `uint` or `long` tag
* **Breaking:** `Drops.BaseFee`, `MedianFee`, `MinimumFee`, `OpenLedgerFee` and `Levels.MedianLevel`, `MinimumLevel`, `OpenLedgerLevel`, `ReferenceLevel` are `ulong`; they were `uint` and failed to read a fee level above 4,294,967,295 under load. Migration: assign to a `ulong` or cast
* `TrustLine.BalanceAsNumber` parses with `Currency.ValueAsNumber`: a balance beyond `decimal` raises `AmountOutOfRangeException` and one below 1e-28 reads as zero, where `decimal.Parse` raised `OverflowException`
* `XummExtension.CheckXummSum` requires six ASCII digits (no sign or whitespace) and a value part of at most 65535
* `RequestManager`: the two "already pending" messages no longer print a stray `$` before the request id
* **Breaking:** `AccountSetAsfFlags.tfInnerBatchTxn` is removed. `tfInnerBatchTxn` (`0x40000000`) is a global flag of the `Flags` field, not an `asf` value of `SetFlag`/`ClearFlag`, and `Validation.ValidateAccountSet` accepted it there. Migration: set `tfInnerBatchTxn` in `Flags` (`XrplGlobalFlags.tfInnerBatchTxn`); `BatchUtils.ToBatchTx` already does
* **Breaking:** `Validation.ValidateMPTokenIssuanceCreate` accepts an `AssetScale` up to 255, the range of its UInt8 type; it refused anything above 10, which neither rippled's preflight nor xrpl.org imposes. `MPTokenIssuanceCreate.MPT_MAX_ASSET_SCALE` is removed. `IMPTokenIssuanceCreate.AssetScale`, `MPTokenIssuanceCreate.AssetScale` and `MPTokenIssuanceCreateResponse.AssetScale` are `byte?`, as `LOMPTokenIssuance.AssetScale` already was; they were `uint?`, which let a value outside the UInt8 range through until the codec refused it at signing. Migration: a literal still compiles; cast a `uint` or `int` variable to `byte`, and read the property into a `byte?` or widen it. `Common.TryGetUInt32` also reads `byte`, `ushort`, `sbyte` and `short`
* `DomainAccessSugar.GetDomainAccess(client, account, domainId, DomainAccessScope, cancellationToken)` and `DomainAccessScope`: `Credentials` is rippled's `credentials::validDomain` (vaults, MPT issuances and other domain-gated objects), `PermissionedDex` is `permissioned_dex::accountInDomain`, which puts the domain owner in its domain without a credential. `DomainAccessResult.IsDomainOwner` reports that case. The existing overload keeps the `Credentials` check
* `RippleStateExtensions` (`Xrpl.Models.Ledger`): trust line helpers from one account's side. `IsLowSide`, `GetPeerOf`, `GetLimitOf`, `GetPeerLimitOf`, `GetBalanceFor` (saturating `decimal`), `GetBalanceValueFor` (the ledger's text, sign flipped for the High side), `IsReserveSetFor`, `IsPeerReserveSetFor`, `GetFlagOf`, `GetPeerFlagOf`, `GetQualityInOf`, `GetQualityOutOf`, `GetPeerQualityInOf`, `GetPeerQualityOutOf`, `OverlayPreviousFields` (`PreviousFields` over `FinalFields`, `Flags = 0` is a value and wins, null is "unchanged") and `IsFlagSet(this RippleStateFlags?, flag)` (null reads as not set). The side is read from the issuers of `LowLimit`/`HighLimit`, which rippled sets to the accounts of the two sides; an account on neither side raises `ArgumentException`. `CurrencyExtensions.ToDecimalClamped(Currency)` reads an amount as `ValueAsNumber` does but answers `decimal.MinValue`/`MaxValue` instead of throwing `AmountOutOfRangeException`. Tests: `TestURippleStateExtensions` in `Xrpl.Tests`
* `Xrpl.BinaryCodec` and `Xrpl.Keypairs` are 11.22.0.0
* Tests: `TestURequestManagerIdReuse` (the continuation is not run on the receive thread; the same request can be sent again from the continuation after `Resolve` and after `Reject`; `GetBalances` sends each page with a new id), `TestUSubmitMultiAndDeleteBlockers`, `TestUBookIndexObserve` (the XRP/CNY book of a mainnet `OfferCreate`, a directory with both sides absent), `TestUModelRegressions` (`EscrowFinish` constructor, XLS-16 codes with multi-byte and ASCII text), `TestUMnemonic.TestUHighFirstByteKey_SignsVerifiably`, `TestUKeypairs.TestUSignSECP_RawKeyWithHighFirstByte`. `TestUMnemonic` expects the prefixed private key. `TestURequestDuringServerSwitch.TestRequestIssuedWhileDisconnectingFailsFast` captures the client for its continuation, which now runs on the thread pool
* Tests: `TestUMinorFixes` in `Xrpl.Tests` (currency, features request, ledger space, batch flags, ledger sequence, `IsSigned`, `Paths`, cross-chain payment, `DIDSet`, NFToken accounts, `ToBatchTx` copy, metadata encoding, X-address tag, fee fields, trust line balance, XUMM numbers, Unix timestamps), `TestUAccountSet` (`SetFlag` `0x40000000`), `TestUMPTokenIssuanceCreate.TestVerifyAssetScaleAboveTen` (11 and 255; `TestThrowsWithAssetScaleOutOfRange` now uses 256) and `TestUAssetScale_IsAUInt8_OnTheModel`, `TestUGetDomainAccess` (domain owner under both scopes); `TestIDomainAccess` checks the owner and a trader under `PermissionedDex`, `TestUMinorFixes` in `Xrpl.BinaryCodec.Test` (MPT amounts, `DecimalValue`, `Uint32.FromJson`, `Hash`, `EncodeVl`, `ReadOrdinal`) and `TestUEcdsaDer` in `Xrpl.Keypairs.Test`. `TestUDIDSet.TestVerify_Invalid_EmptyData` is now `TestVerify_Valid_EmptyDataClearsTheField`, and `TestVerify_Invalid_AllFieldsPresentAndEmpty` is added

## Xrpl.X402 1.0.2 28/10/2026

* `X402PaymentHandler` passes the request's `CancellationToken` to the request-body buffering and to the clone for the retry, and disposes the 402 response when negotiation throws
* `X402PaymentHandler` walks the `exact` requirements of the challenge on its network and pays the first one the client policy allows (`PayToAllowlist`, `MaxAmountDrops`, `IouValueCaps`) and a payment can be built for; when none passes it rethrows the first error. It enforced the policy on the first `exact` option only
* `X402Base64Json.Decode` wraps a `JsonException` in the `FormatException` its documentation promises
* `PaymentHandlerSelectionTests` covers the requirement choice, an option without `extra.invoiceId` ahead of a valid one, the rethrown policy error, the disposed 402 response and the `FormatException`

## 11.21.0.0 26/10/2026

* `definitions.json` is synced with the `server_definitions` of the nightly stand's pinned xrpld build (develop 1f46f75): transaction types `ConfidentialMPTMirrorUpdate` (92), `ConfidentialMPTHolderKeyUpdate` (93), `TransactionProposalCancel` (94), `TransactionProposalCreate` (95); ledger entry type `TransactionProposal` (145); fields `ProposalID` (Hash256), `RecoveryKey`, `InitialIssuerEncryptionKey`, `IssuerMirrorEncryptionKey` (Blob), `SigningFor` (AccountID), `YieldUnrealized`, `AssetsDeployed` (Number), `ProposedTransaction` (STObject); results `tefTEMP_DISABLED`, `tefEXCEEDS_LIMIT`, `terTICKET_RESERVED`. The `EngineResult`, `TransactionType`, `LedgerEntryType` and `Field.*` generated files are regenerated
* ConfidentialMPTKeyRotation: `ConfidentialMPTMirrorUpdate` and `ConfidentialMPTHolderKeyUpdate` models with their responses, `ConfidentialMPTHolderKeyUpdateFlags` (`tfHolderKeyRotation`, `tfHolderKeyRecovery`, `tfCancelRecovery`), and validation of the stateless preflight rules (exactly one mode flag and the fields each mode carries; for a mirror update, at least one ciphertext and the proof). `AutofillSugar` applies the confidential fee multiplier to both, and `DelegateSet` refuses to delegate `ConfidentialMPTHolderKeyUpdate`
* Cosign: `TransactionProposalCreate` and `TransactionProposalCancel` models with their responses, and the `LOTransactionProposal` ledger object. `ProposedTransaction` is read through `TransactionRequestConverter`, as Batch inners are. Validation refuses a zero `Expiration`, a proposed transaction with signature fields, a non-empty `SigningPubKey`, or without a `TicketSequence` and `Sequence` 0, and a zero `ProposalID`. `DelegateSet` refuses to delegate either type. `LedgerEntryRequest.TransactionProposal` (`transaction_proposal`) takes a `TransactionProposalQuery` with the proposed transaction's `account` and `ticket_seq`
* `LOMPToken.IssuerMirrorEncryptionKey` and `LOMPToken.RecoveryKey`, `LOMPTokenIssuance.InitialIssuerEncryptionKey`, `LOVault.YieldUnrealized` and `LOVault.AssetsDeployed`, `LOLoanBroker.DomainID`
* `LoanBrokerSet.DomainID` and `LoanBrokerSetFlags.tfLoanBrokerPrivate` (LendingProtocolV1_2); validation refuses a `DomainID` on a new public broker and a zero `DomainID` on a new broker
* SmartEscrow transaction fields: `EscrowCreate.Bytecode` and `EscrowCreate.Data`, `EscrowFinish.Gas`, `SetFee.GasLimit`, `SetFee.BytecodeSizeLimit` and `SetFee.GasPrice`, with their `TxFormat` entries
* `TxFormat` gains the four new transaction types and `LoanBrokerSet.DomainID`
* Tests: the vendored `transactions.macro` and `ledger_entries.macro` move to develop 1f46f75. `TestUTxFormatConformance` skips the eight XChain types that develop removed (`RemovedOnDevelopOnly`) and fails if one comes back. `TestUKeyRotationAndProposal` covers binary round trips, validation, the `TransactionProposalCreate` response and the `TransactionProposal` object. `TestUAutofillFees.TestUCalculateFee_KeyRotation_AppliesConfidentialMultiplier` and `Serialize_LedgerEntryRequest_TransactionProposal_UsesRippledParameterNames` are added. Integration: `TestITransactionProposal` (create, find through `ledger_entry` and `account_objects`, cancel; cancel of an unknown proposal) and `TestIConfidentialMPTKeyRotation` (domain verdicts for `ConfidentialMPTHolderKeyUpdate` and `ConfidentialMPTMirrorUpdate`), gated by the new `AmendmentGuard.RequireTransactionTypeAsync` and `AmendmentGuard.SkipIfDisabled`, since a Supported::No amendment enabled as a `[features]` preset is invisible to the on-ledger check
* **Breaking:** `XrplClient` caps the fee at `XrplClient.DefaultMaxFeeXRP` (`"2"` XRP, as xrpl.js `DEFAULT_MAX_FEE_XRP`) when `ClientOptions.maxFeeXRP` is not set (#321); `maxFeeXRP` was null and `GetFeeXrp` and `Autofill` filled `base_fee × load_factor × feeCushion` unbounded. What breaks: under a load factor that puts the fee above 2 XRP, the autofilled fee is now 2 XRP. Migration: set `ClientOptions.maxFeeXRP` to a larger value, or to an empty string for no cap; `ChangeServer` with options applies them again, so a cap cleared on the client returns to 2 XRP unless the new options carry the empty string. An owner-reserve charge is paid on top of the cap
* `AutofillSugar` prices the owner-reserve charge of `AccountDelete`, `AMMCreate` and `LedgerStateFix`, and of each such Batch inner, at `reserve_inc × load_factor` rounded up to whole drops (#324): rippled's `Transactor::checkFee` compares the fee with `scaleFeeLoad(calculateBaseFee)`, so the reserve at face value was refused with `telINSUF_FEE_P` under load. `feeCushion` is not applied to the reserve. The cap bounds what the load factor adds and allows one unscaled reserve per reserve charge on top: the fee is at most `maxFeeXRP` plus the reserve for a reserve-priced transaction, or plus one reserve per reserve-priced inner for a `Batch`. Reserve-priced types were exempt from the cap, and a `Batch` with such an inner was capped whole, which fell below rippled's minimum wherever the reserve increment is at or above the cap
* `AutofillSugar` prices a `LoanSet` that is a Batch inner (priced through the Batch, or carrying `tfInnerBatchTxn`) at one base fee plus the signatures present in `CounterpartySignature`, as rippled's `LoanSet::calculateBaseFee` does (#322). It looked up the counterparty's signer list and charged at least one extra base fee, although the counterparty authorises through its `BatchSigner`, which the Batch already charges. A top-level `LoanSet` keeps the signer-list lookup. The `Flags` reader behind it accepts any flags enum and a `ulong`; it read every enum but `LoanPayFlags` as 0
* `AutofillSugar` prices an `EscrowFinish` with `Fulfillment` at `fee × (33 + floor(bytes / 16))`, dividing the size in integers as rippled's `EscrowFinish::calculateBaseFee` does (#322); the fractional quotient charged up to one base fee more when the size was not a multiple of 16 bytes
* Tests: `TestUFeeRoundingAndCulture.TestUXrplClient_MaxFeeNotSet_DefaultsToTwoXrp` and `TestUDefaultCap_HighLoadFactor_HoldsTheFeeAtTwoXrp`, `TestUXrplClient_EmptyMaxFee_LeavesTheFeeUncapped`; `TestUAutofillFees` adds `TestUCalculateFee_EscrowFinishWithFulfillment_DividesSizeLikeRippled` (15, 16, 17 and 32 bytes), `TestUCalculateFee_Batch_InnerLoanSet_CostsBaseFee` (with and without a counterparty signer list), `TestUCalculateFee_Batch_TwoInnerLoanSets_EachCostsBaseFee`, `TestUCalculateFee_LoanSet_InnerBatchFlag_SkipsTheSignerListLookup`, `TestUCalculateFee_LoanSet_InnerBatchFlagAsEnum_SkipsTheSignerListLookup`, `TestUCalculateFee_Batch_WithReserveInner_ReserveIsNotCapped`, `TestUCalculateFee_Batch_WithReserveInner_CapsTheLoadScaledPart`, `TestUCalculateFee_Batch_WithReserveInner_ScalesTheReserveByLoadFactor`, `TestUCalculateFee_ReservePriced_ScalesByLoadFactor` (load factor 2 and 1.03515625), `TestUCalculateFee_ReservePriced_CushionIsNotApplied` and `TestUCalculateFee_ReservePriced_CapBoundsOnlyTheLoadSurcharge`. `TestUCalculateFee_EscrowFinishWithFulfillment_UsesFormula` and `TestAutofill` expect 396 drops (were 399), `TestUCalculateFee_Batch_InnerEscrowFinishWithFulfillment_UsesItsOwnFormula` 444 (was 447)

## 11.20.1.0 26/10/2026

* `AutofillSugar` prices a `Batch` the way rippled's `Batch::calculateBaseFeeImpl` does (#316): one base fee for batch processing, one for the outer transaction, each inner transaction through its own per-type rule (`EscrowFinish` with `Fulfillment`, the reserve-fee types, `LoanSet`, `LoanPay`, confidential MPT, sponsor multisig signers), and one base fee per `BatchSigners` signature. Signatures already present are counted as rippled counts them (one per `TxnSignature`, one per nested `Signers` entry); each required account from `GetBatchSignerAccounts` that has not signed yet counts as one. It charged 3 base fees plus one base fee (or the reserve fee) per inner, which underpriced an `EscrowFinish` with a fulfillment and any batch with more than one co-signer. An inner `Batch` or an inner without `TransactionType` raises `ValidationException`. `BATCH_BASE_FEE_MULTIPLIER` is removed. A participant that multi-signs its `BatchSigner` still needs a `Fee` set by hand
* `XrplWallet.SignAsBatchPart` builds the batch preimage with the ticket number when the outer `Batch` has `Sequence` 0 (or none) and a `TicketSequence`, as rippled's `STTx::getSeqProxy().value()` does (#316); it used 0, so every `BatchSigners` signature for a ticketed outer failed with `Invalid signature`. The serialized `Sequence` stays 0. A `Sequence` or `TicketSequence` that is not a UInt32 raises `ValidationException`
* Tests: `TestUAutofillFees` adds an inner `EscrowFinish` with a fulfillment, present single and multi-signed `BatchSigners` plus a missing one, and a nested `Batch`; the two existing Batch cases expect the BatchSigners fee. `TestUBatchSigningV11.TestUSignAsBatchPart_TicketSequence_SerializesSequenceZero` expects the ticket number in the preimage, and `TestUSignAsBatchPart_SequenceZeroAndTicket_SignsOverTicketNumber` is added. `TestIBatch.TestBatchMultiAccounts_OuterOnTicket` submits a co-signed batch on a ticketed outer, and `TestBatchIndependent_EscrowFinishWithFulfillment_AutofilledFeeSuffices` finishes 8 escrows with fulfillments in one `tfIndependent` batch at the autofilled fee

## 11.20.0.0 25/10/2026

* `XrplAddressCodec.DecodeXAddress` verifies the Base58Check checksum and the 35-byte length and raises `AddressCodecException` on a mismatch, so `IsValidXAddress` is false and `XAddressToClassicAddress` throws for a mistyped X-address; it decoded the typo to another account or tag. An invalid Base58 character also raises `AddressCodecException` (was `EncodingFormatException`); the `EncodingFormatException` is its `InnerException`, through the new `AddressCodecException(string, Exception)` constructor. `B58.DecodeAndCheck` is internal instead of private
* `XrplBinaryCodec.EncodeForSigningClaim` reads the claim amount as a `ulong` (`NumberStyles.None`, invariant culture): an amount above 2,147,483,647 drops threw `OverflowException`, which broke `SignPaymentChannelClaim` and `VerifyPaymentChannelClaim` for claims above ~2147 XRP. The amount may be a JSON string or a JSON number; anything else, a sign, whitespace, a fraction or an exponent raises `ArgumentException`. `int.Parse` accepted `"+5"` and `" 5"`, which are now rejected
* **Breaking:** `XrplKeypairs.DeriveKeypair` honours `algorithm`, as xrpl.js does (`algorithm ?? decoded.Type`; anything but `ed25519` is secp256k1); it always followed the seed's prefix. What breaks: `XrplWallet.FromSeed(seed, masterAddress, algorithm)` with an algorithm that differs from the seed's own now returns that algorithm's keys and address. Migration: pass `null` to keep deriving by the seed prefix
* **Breaking:** `BufferParser.ReadUIntN` and `StreamParser.ReadUIntN` return `uint`; they returned the result cast to a `byte`, so n > 1 lost every byte but the last. Migration: cast the result where a `byte` was assigned
* `StreamParser.Peek` no longer consumes the byte; `Amount.FromParser` read through `StReader.FromStream` lost the first byte of every Amount. At the end of the input `StreamParser.Peek` and `BufferParser.Peek` raise `InvalidOperationException` with the position and size (`BufferParser.Peek` raised `IndexOutOfRangeException`)
* `ShaMapInner` copy-on-write after `ShaMap.Copy`: `PathToIndex` decides on the root's `DoCoW` instead of the deepest inner's, and reads the root after `MoveNext`. Adding to a copied map threw `NullReferenceException` when the copy-on-write path ran, and otherwise mutated inner nodes the two maps share
* `Uint16.FromValue(int)` and `Uint32.FromValue(int)` keep the high bytes and raise `BinaryCodecException` out of range; they truncated the value to a byte. `Uint64.FromValue(string)` pads the hex on the left (`"FF"` is 255), as xrpl.js does; it padded on the right. `Uint64.FromValue(int)` raises `BinaryCodecException` for a negative value, which it wrapped to a large unsigned one
* `ExtensionHelpers.Exponent` and `NumFuncAll(...).Exponent` include the fraction and the written exponent (`"1.5"` is -1, `"1e5"` is 5); they returned the count of trailing zeros
* **Breaking:** `Bits.ToString` overloads print the bytes in order; with the default `Bits.IsLittleEndian = false` they printed them reversed. What breaks: a caller that relied on the reversed string gets the bytes in array order. Migration: reverse the array before the call where the reversed output was wanted
* `RIPEMD160Managed.HashCore` reads full 64-byte blocks from `ibStart`, not from the start of the array
* `Xrpl.AddressCodec`, `Xrpl.BinaryCodec` and `Xrpl.Keypairs` are 11.20.0.0
* Tests: `TestUAddressCodec` adds the xrpl.js bad-checksum and bad-prefix X-addresses, a one-character typo in the account bytes and a 34- and 36-byte payload with a valid checksum; `TestUBaseCodecRegressions` (BinaryCodec) covers each codec fix above; `TestUKeypairs` derives the xrpl.js `sh1HiK7SwjS1VxFdXi7qeMHRedrYX` vector as ed25519 and secp256k1 and an ed25519 seed as secp256k1; `TestURipemd160` hashes the reference 80-byte vector whole and from an offset. `TestUInt.TestFromValue64` and `TestUSeed.TestEDAlgorithmSeed` expected the old behaviour and now expect the xrpl.js result
* `XrpConversion.XrpToDrops` accepts at most 6 fractional digits (was 7) and throws `ValidationException` for an amount that is not a whole number of drops instead of truncating it. **Breaking:** `"1.0000005"` used to convert to `"1000000"`; it now throws, and so does `SignPaymentChannelClaim` / `VerifyPaymentChannelClaim` with such an amount. Round the amount to 6 decimals before converting
* `XrpConversion.XrpToDrops(double)` formats the value with `InvariantCulture`, so it no longer throws `FormatException` under a culture with a decimal comma; `XrpConversion.DropsToXrp(string)` returns an invariant string (`"1.5"`, not `"1,5"`)
* `GetFeeXrpSugar.GetFeeXrp` rounds the fee up to whole drops (xrpl.js rounds half-up to 6 decimals) and floors `maxFeeXRP` to whole drops before capping; `maxFeeXRP` is parsed with `InvariantCulture`. `AutofillSugar.CalculateFeePerTransactionType` reads the cap through the same helper (`GetFeeXrpSugar.MaxFeeDrops`) instead of `XrpToDrops`, which now rejects a sub-drop cap such as `"0.0000115"`. **Breaking:** a `maxFeeXRP` below one drop, or one that is not a number, throws `ValidationException` from both: it would floor to a zero fee, and a zero-fee transaction is never applied. A fractional `load_factor` (e.g. 1.03515625) no longer makes `Autofill` throw `ValidationException` from `XrpToDrops`
* `AutofillSugar.Autofill` fetches Sequence, Fee and LastLedgerSequence concurrently without writing to the transaction, and writes the results after `Task.WhenAll`; the helpers used to add their field to the same `Dictionary` from concurrent continuations. Batch normalization (`BatchNormalizer.NormalizeBatchTransaction`) runs before the fetches instead of alongside them. A non-Batch transaction with `TicketSequence` and no `Sequence` gets `Sequence` 0 instead of the account sequence, which rippled rejects with `temSEQ_AND_TICKET`; account_info is not requested. xrpl.js fills the account sequence in this case. `SetNextValidSequenceNumber`, `CalculateFeePerTransactionType` and `SetLatestValidatedLedgerSequence` keep their signatures and behaviour
* `BalancesSugar.GetXrpBalance` formats the balance with `InvariantCulture`
* Tests: `TestUFeeRoundingAndCulture` covers `GetFeeXrp` and `CalculateFeePerTransactionType` with load factor 1.03515625, a sub-drop `maxFeeXRP`, `Autofill` filling all three fields and dropping a `LastLedgerSequence` of 0, a sub-drop cap through `CalculateFeePerTransactionType` and `Autofill`, a cap below one drop refused, `Sequence` 0 with `TicketSequence` and the account sequence without it, 7-digit rejection in `XrpToDrops`, and `XrpToDrops(double)`, `DropsToXrp`, `GetXrpBalance` and `GetFeeXrp` under a decimal-comma culture. `FeeTestClient` takes `LoadFactor` and `AccountRoot`; `TestUAutofillFees.TestUCalculateFee_DevnetMultiplier_AppliesCorrection` expects 24 drops (1.2 drops rounded up to 2, times 12)
* `BatchNormalizer.NormalizeBatchTransaction` (reached from `Autofill` for a `Batch`) keeps a caller's inner `Sequence` and continues that account's later inner transactions after it; an inner transaction with `TicketSequence` gets `Sequence` 0 and consumes no sequence; an outer `Batch` with `TicketSequence` gets `Sequence` 0 instead of the account's next sequence, so its inner transactions start from the account's ledger `Sequence`. It used to overwrite every inner `Sequence` and fill the outer one next to a `TicketSequence`, which rippled answers with `temSEQ_AND_TICKET`. An inner `Sequence` the caller set below one already assigned does not move the account back, one set before the account was looked up does not stop the lookup, and a `Sequence` of `uint.MaxValue` with a transaction to follow raises `ValidationException` instead of `OverflowException`
* `BatchSigningHelper.MergeBatchSigner` copies an incoming single signature into a `BatchSigner` that has none, and raises `InvalidOperationException` for two different signatures of one account, a single signature for an account that is multi-signed, and a `Signers` list for an account that is single-signed; it used to keep the target as it was, or drop its single signature for the list. Inner `Signers` are merged by signer account: the same signature once, a different signature from the same signer raises `InvalidOperationException` instead of being kept beside the first (rippled answers a duplicate signer with `temBAD_SIGNER`)
* `Common.IsMemo` and `Common.IsSigner` read the STArray form `{"Memo": {...}}` / `{"Signer": {...}}`; `IsMemo` treats `MemoType`, `MemoData` and `MemoFormat` as optional hex strings. `ValidateBaseTransaction`, and with it `Validation.Validate` on the outer `Batch` in `BatchUtils.Build`, rejected every transaction with `Memos` or `Signers`
* `Flags.SetTransactionFlagsToNumber` keeps `int`, `long` and `ulong` flags as `uint` and raises `ValidationException` for a value outside UInt32; it set them to 0, which zeroed the outer `Batch` mode flag. `Flags.ConvertAccountSetFlagsToNumber` maps the `tf*` AccountSet flags (`tfRequireDestTag` ... `tfAllowXRP`) instead of the `asf*` values
* `Common.IsMPTAmount` added; `Common.IsAmount` accepts an MPT amount and `Common.IsIssue` an MPT issue (`mpt_issuance_id` alone, 48 hex characters), as xrpl.js does, so the AMM validators (`AMMCreate`, `AMMDeposit`, `AMMWithdraw`, `AMMDelete`, `AMMBid`, `AMMVote`, `AMMClawback`) accept MPT assets. `Validation.ValidateClawBack` accepts an MPT `Amount`, which requires `Holder` other than `Account`, and rejects `Holder` with a trust-line `Amount`, as rippled's `Clawback` preflight does
* `Validation.ValidateAMMDelete` checks `Asset` and `Asset2` as an Issue (`currency`, `issuer`) instead of an Amount; `Validation.CheckPartialPayment` tests the `tfPartialPayment` bit of `PaymentFlags` and numeric `Flags` instead of comparing for equality; `Validation.ValidateSignerListSet` accepts `SignerQuorum` 0 without `SignerEntries` (deleting the list) and rejects `SignerEntries` with `SignerQuorum` 0
* Tests: `TestUBatchNormalizerSequences` (ticketed outer and inner, caller `Sequence` above and below the counter and the ledger, `uint.MaxValue`), `TestUMergeBatchSigner` (single and multi-signature conflicts, inner signer dedupe), `TestUValidatorRules` (wrapped `Memos` and `Signers`, a `Batch` with both passing `Validation.Validate`, numeric and AccountSet flags, partial payment, signer-list deletion, AMMDelete issues, MPT issues in `IsIssue`, AMMCreate/AMMDeposit/AMMDelete with an MPT asset, MPT clawback); `TestUClawback.TestValidWithHolderForMPT` uses an MPT `Amount`
* Breaking: `LOAmm.AuctionSlot` is an `AMMAuctionSlot` and `LOAmm.VoteSlots` a `List<AMMVoteEntry>`, the ledger entry's own shape (`Account`, `AuthAccounts`, `DiscountedFee`, `Expiration` in Ripple-epoch seconds, `Price`; `Account`, `TradingFee`, `VoteWeight`). They read `AuctionSlot` / `AuthAccounts` / `VoteSlots` as `ledger_entry` and transaction metadata write them, where the `amm_info` models (`AuctionSlot`, `VoteEntry`, `AuthAccount`) lost `DiscountedFee`, every authorized account and every vote. `AMMAuctionSlot.AuthAccounts` is a `List<AuthAccountEntry>`, the type `AMMBid` uses; `AMMAuctionSlot` has no `TimeInterval`, which only `amm_info` reports. Migration: code reading `LOAmm.AuctionSlot.AuthAccounts[i].Account`, `.DiscountedFee`, `.Expiration`, `.Price` and `LOAmm.VoteSlots[i]` members compiles unchanged except where it names the old types (`AuctionSlot`, `VoteEntry`, `AuthAccount` become `AMMAuctionSlot`, `AMMVoteEntry`, `AuthAccountEntry`); code reading `LOAmm.AuctionSlot.TimeInterval` must call `amm_info` (`AMMInfo.AuctionSlot.TimeInterval`) instead, since the ledger entry does not store it; `AMMInfo` is untouched
* `LOAmendments.Majorities` and `LONegativeUNL.DisabledValidators` unwrap the `Majority` / `DisabledValidator` inner objects, so their elements are no longer empty. `InnerObjectListConverter<T>` reads such an STArray as a list of the inner objects, accepts flat elements too, and writes the wrappers back (`MajorityListConverter`, `DisabledValidatorListConverter`, `AMMVoteEntryListConverter`, `AuthAccountEntryListConverter`)
* `RippleDateTimeConverter` converts a `DateTimeKind.Local` value to UTC before writing it, so `DateTime.Now` in `Expiration`, `FinishAfter` or `CancelAfter` is no longer off by the UTC offset. `DateTimeUtils.ISOTimeToRippleTime(DateTime)` does the same, and `ISOTimeToRippleTime(string)` parses straight to UTC
* `RippleResponseWarning.Details` keeps non-string values as their raw JSON text (`WarningDetailsConverter`): warning 1001 has a numeric `expected_date`, and the response failed to parse. Serializing a warning writes those values back as the JSON tokens they were read as; a value replaced by the caller, or a dictionary built by hand, is written as strings
* `HashLedger.HashSignedTx` throws `ValidationException` for a transaction without `TxnSignature`, `Signers`, `SigningPubKey`, `BatchSigners`, `CounterpartySignature` and `SponsorSignature` that is not an inner Batch transaction; the guard was constructed and never thrown
* `LedgerIndexConverter` reads a numeric string as a ledger index instead of an undefined `LedgerIndexType`, and rejects a string that is neither
* `Currency.Equals` compares `MPTokenIssuanceID` (ignoring case), so different MPTs are no longer equal; `Currency.GetHashCode` is overridden consistently with it. `CurrencyExtensions.ToIssued` keeps the MPT issuance ID
* `OrderBookStream.TxJson` holds the API v2 `tx_json` envelope; `OrderBookStream.Transaction` reads it, else `TransactionJson`, and is null when neither is present instead of throwing
* `ShaMap.InnerNode.GetNode` returns null for an empty slot, so `SHAMap.AddItem` and `InnerNode.Hash` work
* `Hashes.HashTX` is `[Obsolete]`: it duplicates `Hashes.HashTx`, the single-signing hash xrpl.js `hashTx` also returns, not the transaction ID; `HashTx` documents that and points to `HashLedger.HashSignedTx`. `InnerObjectListConverter<T>` throws `JsonException` when writing a null element
* Tests: `TestULedgerInnerObjectArrays` (AMM entry from `ledger_entry` and from a metadata `ModifiedNode`, Amendments, NegativeUNL, flat elements, round trip), `TestUTimeConversionLocalZone` and `TestURippleDateTimeConverterLocalZone` under substituted local zones (`LocalTimeZoneScope`), `TestUResponseWarnings`, `TestUHashLedger`, `TestUCurrencyIdentity`, `TestUOrderBookStreamEnvelope`, `TestUSHAMap` (the xrpl.js vector), `TestUHashTx` (the xrpl.js `offerCreateSell` transaction ID) and numeric-string cases in `TestULedgerIndexConverter`
* `LoanManagement` computes a loan broker's `LoanManage` offline the way rippled 3.4.1's `LoanManage` transactor does (#313). `Exposure` is `loanVaultExposure`: `PrincipalOutstanding` for a cash-basis vault (`LEVersion` 1), `TotalValueOutstanding` less `ManagementFeeOutstanding` otherwise. `Availability` returns a `LoanManageAvailability` with a `LoanManageCheck` for impair, unimpair and default - the refusal (`tecNO_PERMISSION`, `tecTOO_SOON`) in the node's order of checks and `AllowedAt`, the first parent close time the time condition holds at (`ImpairableAt`, `DefaultableAt`), exclusive under `fixCleanup3_4_0` and inclusive before it. `Impair`, `Unimpair` and `Default` return a `LoanManageQuote`: the vault's `AssetsTotal`, `AssetsAvailable` and `LossUnrealized` before and after, `DefaultCovered`, `VaultLoss`, the broker's `DebtTotal` and `CoverAvailable` on a default, `NextPaymentDueDateAfter`, the share value before and after when the shares outstanding are given, and the refusal (`tecLIMIT_EXCEEDED` for an impairment past the assets lent out). A vault with `LEVersion` above 1 (rippled develop's `FixedPrecision`) throws `NotSupportedException`
* `LoanManagement` takes a parent close time to whole seconds before the time conditions, as the ledger does: a time inside the second of an exclusive boundary is not past it
* `PreviewSugar.PreviewLoanManage` previews a `LoanManage`; `LoanManageOutcome.FromMetadata` reads the action, the loan's flags and the vault and broker deltas from its metadata, a `Vault` amount rising from zero included. `LoanManageAction` names the action
* Tests: `TestULoanManagement` covers both sides of `fixCleanup3_4_0` at `NextPaymentDueDate` and at `NextPaymentDueDate + GracePeriod`, both accounting bases, XRP, IOU and MPT vaults, an impaired loan defaulted, cover below the liquidation amount, `tecLIMIT_EXCEEDED`, the dust and `tecINTERNAL` branches of a default, a `FixedPrecision` vault, the due date before the fix, the owner check and the outcome read from metadata. `TestILoanManagement` impairs, unimpairs, impairs again and defaults an XRP and an IOU loan on the stand, comparing each quote with the preview and the submitted metadata

## Xrpl.X402.AspNetCore 1.1.0 25/10/2026

* `LedgerSettlingFacilitator.VerifyAndSettleAsync` checks the signed transaction against `envelope.Accepted` before submitting it; it checked only `Destination`. The blob must be a `Payment` (`invalid_transaction_type`) to `PayTo` (`invalid_destination`) without `tfPartialPayment` (`invalid_partial_payment`), its `Amount` in the required asset - XRP drops, or the currency issued by `extra.issuer`, with the code as text or 40 hex digits; an MPT amount is refused (`invalid_asset`) - and exactly the required amount (`invalid_amount`), with a `LastLedgerSequence` (`invalid_last_ledger_sequence`) that is not yet validated (`payment_expired`). After validation the metadata's `delivered_amount` must be the required asset and at least the required amount (`delivered_amount_mismatch`, with the transaction hash, network and payer); a `tes` result alone was accepted
* `LedgerSettlingFacilitator` refuses a transaction hash it already settled or is settling as `duplicate_payment`. Resubmitting a validated blob returns `tefPAST_SEQ` or `tefALREADY`, which `SubmitRequestAndWait` follows to the original `tesSUCCESS`, so the same `PAYMENT-SIGNATURE` unlocked the resource again. A settlement that ended `settlement_failed`, `settlement_unknown` or `settlement_error` is released, so the same blob can be presented again
* New `ISettledPaymentStore` (`TryReserveAsync`, `ReleaseAsync`) and its process-local default `InMemorySettledPaymentStore`, which keeps a hash until the validated ledger passes the payment's `LastLedgerSequence` by `RetentionMarginLedgers` (default 256). New constructor `LedgerSettlingFacilitator(IXrplClient, ISettledPaymentStore)`; `LedgerSettlingFacilitator(IXrplClient)` uses its own in-memory store. New `X402ServiceCollectionExtensions.AddLedgerSettlingFacilitator()` registers the facilitator as the singleton `IX402Facilitator` with the in-memory store unless an `ISettledPaymentStore` is registered first. Several instances settling for one merchant must share a store
* `LedgerSettlingFacilitator` reads the validated ledger (`GetLedgerIndex`) before each settlement, and refuses a `LastLedgerSequence` more than `MaxTimeoutSeconds` / 3 ledgers past it, at least 20, plus 10 for a payer whose node is ahead of the facilitator's (`invalid_last_ledger_sequence`), which bounds how long a hash is kept. A store whose `ReleaseAsync` throws no longer replaces the response with `settlement_error`; `ISettledPaymentStore.TryReserveAsync` documents that an implementation that throws must leave the hash unclaimed
* `LedgerSettlingFacilitator` requires `extra.invoiceId`, when present, to be bound into the transaction as `InvoiceID` = SHA-256 of its UTF-8 bytes or as a memo whose `MemoData` is those bytes in hex (`invalid_invoice_id`); an X-address `PayTo` or `extra.destinationTag` requires that `DestinationTag` (`invalid_destination`)
* `RequirePayment` hands the facilitator the endpoint's own requirement instead of the client's `accepted`, and also compares `extra.issuer`, `extra.invoiceId` and `extra.destinationTag`; the client's `issuer` decided which issued currency was accepted. `Asset` is compared exactly, as currency codes are case-sensitive; it was compared ignoring case. `IX402Facilitator` documents that `Accepted` must be the server's requirement
* `ISettledPaymentStore` documents that a store serves one network and that instances sharing it should read the validated ledger from nodes in step
* READMEs of `Xrpl.X402` and `Xrpl.X402.AspNetCore` and `X402-Guide` (both languages) list the checks and the error reasons, describe the store, the shared instance and `AddLedgerSettlingFacilitator`, and keep one facilitator for every endpoint
* Tests: `LedgerSettlingFacilitatorTests` refuses a `CheckCreate`, another destination, a partial payment, another currency, another issuer, a token for XRP and XRP for a token, a short amount in drops and in a token, a missing and a passed `LastLedgerSequence`, a short, foreign or missing `delivered_amount`, and a replayed or concurrently presented blob, alone and across facilitators sharing a store; it accepts the currency written as text or hex and other flags, presents again a blob whose settlement failed or stayed unknown, prunes the in-memory store past the margin, and resolves the services `AddLedgerSettlingFacilitator` registers. It also binds the invoice by `InvoiceID` or memo and refuses an unbound or other invoice, requires the tag of an X-address `PayTo` or `extra.destinationTag`, bounds the window (`uint.MaxValue`, past the autofill and signer windows) and accepts one ending in the next ledger, compares token values `1`, `1.0`, `1e0` and `0.1e1` as equal, and refuses an MPT `Amount` and an `unavailable` or MPT `delivered_amount`. `RequirePaymentTests` refuses another issuer, another invoice and a differently cased asset without settling, and checks the facilitator sees the server's requirement

## 11.19.0.0 24/10/2026

* `DexSnapshot.WithoutOffer(string offerIndex)` returns the snapshot without one offer, its `DexIndex` derived from the snapshot's (`SnapshotRoll.WithoutOffer`, the `DexIndexDelta` the roll uses) instead of built at the next evaluation (#310). The index is matched in either case; a snapshot that does not hold the offer is returned as it is. Only `Offers` changes: owner counts, sequences and balances are left alone. `DexSnapshot.WithoutOffer(string offerIndex, bool releaseOwnerReserve)` also lowers the owner's `OwnerCount` by one, never below zero and only for an owner the snapshot lists, with the account patched into the derived index; `SnapshotRoll.PatchAccounts` takes the `DexIndex` instead of the `DexWorld`
* Docs: `Amounts-Guide` (both languages) describes `WithoutOffer` and `releaseOwnerReserve` for an `OfferCancel` and for taking out a placed remainder
* Tests: `TestUSnapshotRoll` takes out a placed remainder that was the only offer of its book, an offer mid-book, an offer named in lower case and a hybrid offer from both its books, comparing each derived index with one built afresh and the next result through it with one through a fresh index; an offer the snapshot does not hold returns the same instance. With `releaseOwnerReserve` it takes out a placed remainder and checks the owner's count is back to the one before the `OfferCreate`, the derived index and the next result, a count already at zero staying at zero, and an owner the snapshot does not list not being added

## 11.18.1.0 24/10/2026

* `OfferCreateCrossing.Cross` finds the offer `OfferSequence` cancels in the snapshot's index instead of scanning every offer of the snapshot (#306). `DexIndex.Offers` compares ledger indexes ignoring case, as the scan did
* `LedgerChanges.Collect` reads only the offers the transaction wrote (`DexView.TouchedOffers`), still in the snapshot's order, instead of reading every offer of the snapshot, which allocated an offer state for each. On a snapshot of 4,000 offers a cancel-and-replace `OfferCreate` takes 0.13 ms instead of 0.46 ms and allocates 35 KiB instead of 658 KiB (p50)
* Tests: `TestUDexGaps` cancels an offer whose index the snapshot spells in lower case, and cancels nothing when the snapshot does not hold the offer or another account owns an offer under its index; `BenchmarkDexWorld` measures a cancel-and-replace
* `LedgerRules.FromNodeAsync` sets `FixFillOrKill` whatever the `feature` command reports: rippled develop retired the amendment (`XRPL_RETIRE_FIX(FillOrKill)`, XRPLF/rippled#7366). Docs: `Amounts-Guide` (both languages) says so; `StandaloneNode-Guide` (both languages) explains how `feature` reports a retired or removed amendment and uses `AMMClawback` as its example
* Tests: `TestULedgerRules.RetiredFixFillOrKillIsOnWhateverTheNodeReports`. `AmendmentGuard` reads AMM, XRPFees, fixFillOrKill, fixAMMOverflowOffer and fixAMMv1_1 as active when `feature` reports them vetoed as obsolete, and `AmendmentGuard.RequireXChainBridgeAsync` skips `TestIBatchInnerTypes.Batch_Inner_XChain_CreateBridge_Modify_ClaimID_Commit_AccountCreateCommit` and `TestISponsoredTypes.Sponsored_XChain_CreateBridge_Modify_ClaimID_Commit_AccountCreateCommit` where the bridge is gone (XRPLF/rippled#8255)
* CI: `.ci-config/wait-genesis-amendments.sh` waits until any `[amendments]` entry of a stand's config is enabled; `dotnet.test.yml`, `release-watch.yml` and `nightly-pin-watch.yml` use it instead of polling the AMM amendment, which a retired AMM never reports enabled

## 11.18.0.0 24/10/2026

* `PaymentFlow.Evaluate` and `OfferCreateCrossing.Cross` index a `DexSnapshot` once per instance and reuse the index for every later evaluation against it (#301); `Cross` indexed it twice per call. The internal `DexWorld` is split into `DexIndex`, the read-only accounts, lines, books, pools, MPTs, domains and credentials, cached per snapshot in a `ConditionalWeakTable`, and `DexWorld`, which keeps what belongs to one evaluation: the fee, the passes, `ReachedPartialBook`, and the accounts a payment creates (`DexAccounts`). `DexSnapshot` keeps its own read-only copy of each list it is given, so changing a list after building the snapshot does not change it; a null list is an empty one
* Tests: `TestUDexIndex` compares results through the shared index with a freshly built one over payments and offers, sequentially and concurrently, and checks that an account a payment creates does not reach the next evaluation; `BenchmarkDexWorld` (manual, outside the `TestU` filter) measures indexing and evaluation on a synthetic snapshot of 4,000 offers and 5,010 trust lines
* `PaymentFlowResult.SnapshotAfter` and `OfferCrossingResult.SnapshotAfter` are the snapshot the transaction leaves, to evaluate the next one against (#301): XRP and trust line balances after the fee, owner counts, the sender's `Sequence` moved on or its `TicketSequence` consumed, trust lines created and deleted, offers crossed or removed, the placed offer under the index `Hashes.HashOfferId` gives it, pool balances (and the pool's own AccountRoot, trust lines and MPTokens when the snapshot lists them), MPTokens and `OutstandingAmount`, and the account a successful payment creates, with the ledger after `DexSnapshot.LedgerSequence` as its `Sequence`. The placed offer's index is made from the transaction's `TicketSequence`, else its `Sequence`, else the account's. A result that is not applied returns the evaluated snapshot. Built on first read; reading it raises `InvalidOperationException` when an offer is placed and no sequence is known. Its `DexIndex` is derived from the evaluated snapshot's (`DexIndexDelta`, `SnapshotRoll`) instead of built afresh: a chain of 15 transactions over 4,000 offers and 5,010 trust lines takes 21 ms instead of 96 ms (p50)
* `OfferCreateCrossing.Cross(DexSnapshot, OfferCreate, LedgerRules)` reads `TicketSequence`
* Docs: evaluating transactions in sequence in `Amounts-Guide` (both languages)
* Tests: `TestISnapshotRoll` evaluates chains of payments and offers offline - through books, and through an AMM pool and books - and submits them to the node, comparing each step's `SnapshotAfter` with the state `FromNodeAsync` reads back: balances, owner counts, sequences, trust lines, pool balances and both books offer by offer with their qualities. `TestUSnapshotRoll` runs chains of payments and offers - through books and a pool, a placed offer tied mid-book and crossed, an account created, a trust line created and deleted, MPTs moved - and checks at each step that the derived index equals one built afresh, that the next result through it equals the one through a fresh index, and that the snapshot holds the result's balance, offer, trust line and pool changes; it also checks the sequence, the ticket, the created account's sequence, that a failed payment leaves no created account, a pool's listed AccountRoot, and the copied properties. `SyntheticDexSnapshot` uses encoded addresses, books that do not cross, and an optional pool; `BenchmarkDexWorld` measures the 15-transaction chain

## 11.17.0.0 21/10/2026

* `VaultPhase` (`None`, `Subscription`, `Investment`, `Redemption`) and `VaultShares.PhaseAt(LOVault, DateTime?, LedgerRules)` are public (#298): the phase of a closed-ended vault as rippled's `getVaultPhase` decides it, with `SubscriptionDate` inside the subscription phase and `RedemptionDate` inside the redemption phase. A vault without `SubscriptionDate` stays in its subscription phase, one without `RedemptionDate` in its investment phase. `PhaseAt` returns `None` for a vault that is not closed-ended, without a time, and when `LendingProtocolV1_1` is not enabled; `Deposit`, `WithdrawAssets` and `RedeemShares` refuse by the phase it returns
* Tests: `TestUVaultShares` checks `PhaseAt` on both sides of `SubscriptionDate` and `RedemptionDate`, with either date missing, with no time, with `LendingProtocolV1_1` off, for an open-ended vault and one without `VaultKind`, and with local and unspecified times

## 11.16.0.0 19/10/2026

* **Breaking:** `LOCheck.SourceTag` is `uint?`, the number rippled sends for the UInt32; it was `string`, which no Check carrying a `SourceTag` could be read into (mainnet CheckCash `E14C0351A673C27F6A1F0795EB7D665339F6B8C7EDB84E812DCA795127F25E1F`). Migration: read the tag as a number
* `LedgerRules.FromNodeAsync` sets `FixAMMv1_1` whatever the `feature` command reports: rippled develop retired the amendment (`XRPL_RETIRE_FIX(AMMv1_1)`, XRPLF/rippled#8226). Docs: `Amounts-Guide` (both languages) says so
* `RippleStateFlags` is a `uint` enum, as the other ledger flag enums are; as an `int` it could not hold a UInt32 `Flags` with the top bit set
* **Breaking:** `TransactionResponseConverter` raises `JsonException` when a transaction cannot be read, with the transaction type and `hash` in the message and the original exception as `InnerException`; it returned the object built from `TransactionType` with every other field empty. What breaks: `TxV1` and `AccountTransactions` raise `XrplException` ("Failed to deserialize response", this `JsonException` as `InnerException`) where they returned a result, a `transaction` stream message goes to `OnError` (`streamHandlerError`, the frame text in `data`) instead of `OnTransaction`, and the `OrderBookStream.Transaction` getter raises the `JsonException`. Migration: catch `XrplException` around those requests and `JsonException` around the getter, and handle `streamHandlerError` in `OnError` to see the transactions a stream could not read
* Tests: `TestUCheckSourceTag` reads the CheckCash above through `TxV1` and `TxV2`; `tx_check_cash_raw.json` and `tx_check_cash_v1_raw.json` join the `TestUResponseFidelity` corpus; `TestULedgerEntryFieldsConformance.TestULedgerEntryModels_ReadIntegerFieldsAsRippledSendsThem` reads every UInt8, UInt16, UInt32, Int32 and UInt64 field of every ledger object model from the value rippled sends at the top of its range; `TestUTransactionResponseConverter` expects the exception instead of the empty object; `TestULedgerRules.RetiredFixAMMv1_1IsOnWhateverTheNodeReports`

## 11.15.0.0 18/10/2026

* `LoanBrokerCover` (`Xrpl.Sugar`) computes a loan broker's first-loss cover offline from the `LoanBroker` and `Vault` entries, as rippled's `LoanBrokerCoverDeposit`, `LoanBrokerCoverWithdraw`, `LoanBrokerCoverClawback` and `minimumBrokerCover` do (#290). `MinimumCover` is the minimum `LoanBrokerCoverWithdraw` keeps: `CoverRateMinimum` of `DebtTotal` rounded up, to the scale of the vault's `AssetsTotal` under `fixCleanup3_2_0` and to the scale of `DebtTotal` before it. `MaxWithdrawable` is the largest withdrawal the node accepts. `CheckDeposit` rounds the deposit down to the scale of `CoverAvailable` under `fixCleanup3_2_0`; `CheckWithdraw` and `CheckClawback` check the amount against the minimum, which a pre-fix clawback does not round to the asset; `MaxClawback` is what a clawback without an amount takes. The checks return `LoanBrokerCoverQuote` (`Amount`, `CoverBefore`, `CoverAfter`, `MinimumCover`, `Refusal`, `RefusalReason`) with `temBAD_AMOUNT`, `tecPRECISION_LOSS`, `tecINSUFFICIENT_FUNDS` or `tecNO_PERMISSION`
* `PreviewSugar.PreviewLoanBrokerCoverDeposit`, `PreviewLoanBrokerCoverWithdraw` and `PreviewLoanBrokerCoverClawback` return `LoanBrokerCoverOutcome` (`CoverBefore`, `CoverAfter`, `MinimumCover`, `MaxWithdrawable`, `BrokerAfter`). The preview reads the broker's vault with `ledger_entry`, and the rules with `LedgerRules.FromNodeAsync` unless they are passed. `LoanBrokerCoverOutcome.FromMetadata` reads the same from a validated transaction
* `AssetRounding.ScaleInMode` (internal) takes `scale()` in the context's rounding mode, as `minimumBrokerCover` does inside its upward guard
* Docs: withdrawing cover in `LendingProtocol-Guide` (both languages)
* Tests: `TestULoanBrokerCover` covers XRP, IOU and MPT vaults on both sides of `fixCleanup3_2_0`, an IOU vault whose `AssetsTotal` scale differs from the `DebtTotal` scale, and a 17-digit `AssetsTotal` that the upward guard takes to the next exponent; `TestILoanBrokerCover` withdraws `MaxWithdrawable` from IOU, XRP and MPT brokers, gets `tecINSUFFICIENT_FUNDS` from the node for one unit more and for the pre-fix maximum as `CheckWithdraw` does, claws back what `CheckClawback` says, and compares each preview with the submitted transaction

## 11.14.1.0 15/10/2026

* `AccountId.FromParser` honours the VL length of an AccountID field (#287). A zero-length value, which rippled writes for a defaulted AccountID such as the `Account` of a `UNLModify` pseudo-transaction, decodes to `""`. `AccountId.FromJson` reads `""` back as the zero-length value, so the field encodes back as `81 00`. A length other than 0 or 20 raises `FormatException`. `Hash160` gains a non-public constructor that admits the empty buffer
* `Uint8.FromJson` and `Uint16.FromJson` read a number from a node built in memory from any CLR numeric type, including the `uint` that `XrplBinaryCodec.Decode` emits for a UInt8 or UInt16 field, and a base-10 string. A value out of range raises `OverflowException`
* Tests: `TestUPseudoTransactions` decodes and round-trips mainnet `UNLModify`, `EnableAmendment` and `SetFee` blobs, the last two with the 20-byte zero account, and encodes the JSON rippled returns for the `UNLModify`; `TestUUintFromJson`

## 11.14.0.0 12/10/2026

* **Breaking:** `BaseAsset` and `QuoteAsset` of `PriceData` (`Xrpl.BinaryCodec` 11.14.0.0) encode a three-character code at bytes 12-14, as rippled and xrpl.js encode every Currency field. The codec wrote it at bytes 0-2, which the ledger holds as a different, nonstandard currency: an oracle published by the SDK with `USD` was shown by the node as `5553440000000000000000000000000000000000`, was not found by `get_aggregate_price` for `USD`, and did not aggregate with oracles published by other SDKs. `Currency.FromOracleJson`, `Currency.FromOracleString` and `Currency.EncodeOracleCurrency` are removed; `Currency.GetCurrencyCodeFromTlcBytes` no longer reads text at bytes 0-2 as a standard code, and `OracleCurrencyConverter` reads such a value as hex instead of as the three characters. Migration: an oracle the SDK published with a three-character code holds the nonstandard code; send `OracleSet` again with the same codes to store the standard ones, and delete the old pairs if they are no longer wanted
* `IXrplClient.GetAggregatePrice` with `GetAggregatePriceRequest`, `OracleIdentifier`, `GetAggregatePriceResponse` and `AggregatePriceSet` (`get_aggregate_price`). `AggregatePriceAssetConverter` writes `base_asset` / `quote_asset` in the form rippled displays the stored currency in, which is what the node compares them with. The time filter is sent as `time_threshold`, the name rippled reads; the xrpl.org reference and xrpl.js name it `trim_threshold`
* `IXrplClient.BookChanges` with `BookChangesRequest` and `BookChangesResponse` (`book_changes`). Without a ledger, rippled answers for the open ledger, not a validated one
* `IXrplClient.Manifest` with `ManifestRequest`, `ManifestResponse` and `ManifestDetails` (`manifest`). For a key with no manifest, rippled answers with details naming the key as both master and ephemeral key, without `manifest` or `seq`; the xrpl.js model expects `details` to be absent instead
* `IXrplClient.Version` with `VersionResponse` and `ApiVersionRange` (`version`). `first`, `good` and `last` are read as strings: rippled sends the strings `1.0.0` to API version 1 requests and the numbers of the supported range to later ones
* `IXrplClient.GetAggregatePrice`, `BookChanges`, `Manifest` and `Version` have default implementations that call `GRequest`, so an existing `IXrplClient` implementation compiles without them
* `PathFindCreateRequest.Domain` (`domain`): the streaming `path_find create` searches a permissioned domain's books, as `RipplePathFindRequest.Domain` does for `ripple_path_find`. Without it, a search for a `Payment` with `DomainID` quoted the open books. `PathFindCreateRequest.SendMax` documents that rippled accepts it only with a `DestinationAmount` of -1
* `BookChange` (the `book_changes` stream and method) gains `MptIssuanceIdA`, `MptIssuanceIdB` and `Domain`, which rippled sends for an MPT side and for a permissioned-domain book; `AssetA` / `AssetB` carry the MPT issuance ID for an MPT side
* `TakerAmount` (`book_offers`) gains `MptIssuanceId` (`mpt_issuance_id`), the MPT side of an order book under MPTokensV2 (XLS-82), and `TakerAmount.FromAsset(IssuedCurrency)`, which builds the side of XRP, an issued currency or an MPT. `DexSnapshotLoader` and `BookIndex` build their `book_offers` sides with it, so an MPT side is asked for by its issuance ID rather than with neither currency nor issuer
* `BookOffersRequest.Validate()` checks a request as rippled develop's `book_offers` handler does, in its order: `mpt_issuance_id` excludes `currency` and `issuer` and is 48 hex digits; `currency` and `issuer` are parsed as `toCurrency` and `toIssuer` parse them, with XRP taking no issuer and an issued currency requiring one other than account one; `taker` is an address, `domain` 64 hex digits; and two sides naming one asset are `badMarket`. Messages name the field and the rippled error code
* **Behaviour change:** `IXrplClient.BookOffers` calls `Validate()` and raises `ValidationException` before sending a request the node would refuse; such a request used to reach the node and fail with `RippledException`
* `OfferCreateCrossing`, `PaymentFlow` and `PathFinding` cross, pay and find paths through MPT order books (MPTokensV2, XLS-82), ported from rippled 3.4.0; the `NotSupportedException` they threw for an MPT side, payment or path step is gone. Ported: `MPTEndpointStep` (`MptEndpointStep`), `toStrand`'s MPT path elements and no-rippling rules, `BookStep`'s MPT transfer rate, `WeakAuth` and `checkMPTDEX` checks, issuer input cap, overflow removal, `checkCreateMPT` and `issuerSelfDebitHookMPT`, `OfferStream`'s `issuerFundsToSelfIssue`, `directSendNoFeeMPT` with `PaymentSandbox`'s `creditMPT`, `balanceHookMPT` and `balanceHookSelfIssueMPT`, `accountHolds` / `accountFunds` / `requireAuth` / `canTrade` / `canTransfer` for MPT, `OfferCreate`'s MPT `preflight` and `preclaim` (`temDISABLED`, `tecLOCKED`, an unfunded MPT issuer, `checkAcceptAsset`, `canTrade`), the tick size leaving an MPT side exact and an unrepresentable quality not placed, `Payment`'s pre-MPTokensV2 direct MPT payment with its transfer fee, `getBookBase`'s tagged MPT book keys, and the path finder's `getMPTs`, `PathFindMPT::canSend`, MPT account links, books and path counts
* `DexSnapshot.MptIssuances` (`DexMptIssuance`) and `DexSnapshot.MptHoldings` (`DexMptHolding`); `DexSnapshot.FromNodeAsync` reads the issuances and MPTokens of the strands, offers and pools it covers, and `UnlimitedTaker` holds plenty of an MPT it pays
* `OfferCrossingResult.MpTokens` and `PaymentFlowResult.MpTokens` (`MpTokenChange`): the MPTokens a transaction creates. `BalanceChanges` report MPT holdings, and an issuer's change as the negated change of `OutstandingAmount`, as `BalanceChanges.GetBalanceChanges` reads the metadata
* `OfferRemovalReason.Overflow`
* `OfferCreateCrossing.RoundToTickSize` leaves an MPT side as given
* `BookIndex` reads MPT book directories (`TakerPaysMPT`, `TakerGetsMPT`), and `BookIndex.FromAccountsAsync` adds the MPTs the accounts issue or hold. `PathFindRequest.SourceCurrencies` names an MPT by `MptIssuanceId`; `PathFindResult.DestinationCurrencies` lists the destination's MPT issuance IDs
