* `SourceCurrency.MptIssuanceId` (`mpt_issuance_id`) for `ripple_path_find`
* Docs: "MPTs on the DEX" in the Amounts guide (`DocFx/Amounts-Guide.md`, `Amounts-Guide.ru.md`)
* CI: `MPTEndpointStep.cpp`, `TokenHelpers.h`, `MPTokenHelpers.h` / `.cpp`, `MPTAmount.h` / `.cpp`, `PathAsset.h`, `Indexes.cpp` and `rpc/detail/MPT.h` are on the engine-watch list of `protocol-watch.yml`
* CI: `.ci-config/check-rpc-methods.sh` with `.ci-config/rpc-methods-ignore.txt`, run by the `rpc-methods` job of `protocol-watch.yml`, compares rippled develop's RPC method names with the SDK's request models, with no method left as a gap; `MethodNames.h` and `handlers/Handlers.h` are on the protocol-watch list. `submit_multisigned` is listed as covered: a multi-signed blob goes through `submit` (`Signer.Multisign`, `SubmitMulti`)
* Tests: `TestUGetAggregatePrice`; `TestIOracle` aggregates prices with trim, with a nonstandard code and with `time_threshold`, and asks for an unpriced pair; `TestUOracleSet` compares the `OracleSet` encoding byte for byte with xrpl.js and replaces `TestVerify_CurrencyOracleEncoding` with `TestVerify_CurrencyEncoding_StandardCodeAtIsoPosition`; `TestUOracleCurrencyConverter.Read_HexCode40Chars_ThreeCharsAtByteZero_StaysHex`; `TestUBookChangesManifestVersion`; `TestURequestSerialization` covers `domain` in `path_find create`; `TestIPathFinding.PathFindCreateThroughADomainBook` checks that `path_find` quotes the domain's book as `ripple_path_find` does; `TestIBookChangesManifestVersion` reads the trade of an offer crossing back through `book_changes`, the manifest of a key without one, and the API version range; `TestISubmitMultisigned`, an empty stub that tested nothing, is removed (multi-signed submission is covered by `TestIMultisign`); `TestUBookOffersMpt`; `TestUMptDex` builds strands as rippled's `PayStrandMPT_test` pins them and crosses and pays MPTs worked out by hand; `TestUOfferCreateCrossing.MptSidesAreNotSupported` is removed; `TestIMptDex` predicts each crossing and payment with `OfferCreateCrossing` and `PaymentFlow` and compares it with the node through `BookCrossingHarness` - offers pairing an MPT with XRP and with an issued currency, the taker's MPToken created, a transfer fee rounded up, a walked MPT book, an issuer's own offer at its maximum, an MPT/XRP pool crossed with the book, a locked issuance, payments from XRP and from an issued currency, with a fee and between holders, a locked sender - reads MPT books through `book_offers`, and sends the requests `Validate()` refuses to the node and gets the same errors back; `TestIPathFinding` compares MPT searches with `ripple_path_find` (XRP to an MPT, an MPT as the source currency, XRP to an issued currency bridged through an MPT book, the holder's MPT among every source asset) and searches at the stand's `[path_search]` of 7, which `ripple_path_find` uses, instead of 2; `BookCrossingHarness.AdjustRules` turns MPTokensV2 on for a standalone stand, where it is a `[features]` preset, and `AssertMpTokens` compares the MPTokens created
* **Breaking (source, named arguments only):** `LoanSigningHelper` names its LoanSet parties by role, submitter (`Account`) and counterparty, instead of broker and borrower (#276). rippled takes the counterparty as `Counterparty`, or the LoanBroker's owner when the field is omitted, and needs one of `Account` and the counterparty to be the owner, so the borrower may submit and the broker owner co-sign. Renamed parameters: `PrepareForSigning(…, submitterWallet)`, `SignLoanSet(…, submitterWallet, counterpartyWallet)`, `CombineLoanSignatures(submitterSignedBlob, counterpartySignedBlob)`. Migration: a call passing these by name uses the new names; positional calls are unchanged
* `LoanSigningHelper.SignLoanSet` takes the two wallets in either order and gives the TxnSignature to the one whose address is `Account`; it refused a borrower-submitted LoanSet whose `Counterparty` is the broker owner. It throws when neither wallet is `Account`, or when the other wallet is not `Counterparty`. The new overload `SignLoanSet(preparedTx, submitterWallet, counterpartyWallet, brokerOwner)` also checks the counterparty when `Counterparty` is omitted and that one of the two parties is the broker owner
* `LoanSigningHelper.SubmitterSign` replaces `BrokerSign`, which is `[Obsolete]` and forwards to it
* Docs: `ILoanSet.Counterparty`, `LoanSet`, `LoanSigningHelper`, `XrplWallet.SignAsLoanCounterparty` and `LendingProtocol-Guide` (both languages) describe the two roles
* Tests: `TestULoanSigningRoles`; `TestILoanBorrowerSubmits` submits a LoanSet from the borrower with `Counterparty` set to the broker owner, with it omitted, and with the broker owner signing first, gated on `AmendmentGuard.LendingProtocol`
* `Validation.ValidateLoanManage` accepts a LoanManage with no action flag, as rippled's `LoanManage::preflight` does: it rejects only more than one of `tfLoanDefault`, `tfLoanImpair` and `tfLoanUnimpair` (`temINVALID_FLAG`), and `doApply` treats none as a no-op (#277). It refused a transaction with `Flags` absent, zero, or carrying only `tfInnerBatchTxn`. `LoanManage`, `LoanManageFlags` and `LendingProtocol-Guide` document the no-op
* Tests: `TestULoanManage`
* **Breaking:** `ITransactionCommon` gains `SponsorSignature` and `ILoanSet` gains `CounterpartySignature`, both `SignatureObject` (#278). `TransactionRequest` and `TransactionResponse` implement the first, `LoanSet` and `LoanSetResponse` the second. A request model dropped either co-signature when deserialized and written back with `ToJson`; a response kept it only as raw JSON in `UnknownFields`, where it no longer appears. Migration: a class implementing `ITransactionCommon` or `ILoanSet` directly adds the property; code reading either field from `UnknownFields` reads the typed property
* `SignatureObjectConverter` writes a `SignatureObject` in its wire shape (`SigningPubKey` and `TxnSignature`, or `Signers` with each entry wrapped in `Signer`) and reads one without enforcing a form, so a Batch inner marker - an empty object, or an empty `SigningPubKey` - writes back as it came. `SignatureObject.ValidateShape` also refuses a `Signer` entry that nests `Signers`, which `FromJsonObject` refused before through its recursive parse. Both fields are not signing fields in the codec, so they change no signing preimage
* `SignatureResult.GetTx` keeps both co-signatures instead of refusing a blob that carries them, so a partially signed LoanSet decodes and its submitter's `Sign` of the typed transaction gives the blob `LoanSigningHelper.SubmitterSign` gives
* Tests: `TestUCoSignatureFields` round-trips both fields in single and multisig form through the request and response models, checks that the signing preimage under the transaction, counterparty and sponsor prefixes is unchanged by them, signs a decoded partially signed LoanSet, and round-trips the Batch inner markers; `TestUSignatureResultRoundTrip` refuses an unmodelled field (`Destination` on a LoanSet) and keeps `CounterpartySignature`
* `Validation.ValidateBatch` (and `BatchUtils.Build`, which calls it) no longer rejects Vault, LoanBroker and Loan inner transactions. rippled develop refuses them (`kDisabledTxTypes` → `temINVALID_INNER_BATCH`) only while `LendingProtocolV1_2` is not enabled (rippled #8244); the offline check has no ledger rules, so the node decides. `DisabledInnerTxTypes` is removed
* Tests: `TestUBatchCoSigning.TestUValidateBatch_LendingInner_LeftToTheNode` (all fifteen types) and `TestUBuildBatch_TypedLoanSetAndVaultDeposit_Builds` replace `TestUValidateBatch_LoanOrVaultInner_Throws`; `TestIBatchInnerTypes.Batch_Inner_VaultCreate_RejectedByNodeBeforeLendingProtocolV12` and `Batch_Inner_VaultCreate_WithLendingProtocolV12`, gated on `AmendmentGuard.LendingProtocolV12`; the Sponsorship guide describes the amendment gate

* `Xrpl.Amounts` ports the integer-arithmetic hardening of rippled 3.4.1 in the payment engine: `StrandFlow.Run` returns `tecPATH_DRY` when an XRP or MPT sum of the strands' ins or outs leaves `int64` (`checkedStepAddOpt`), and `BookCrossingStep` fails the strand with `tecPATH_DRY` when its sum does (`checkedStepAdd`). New: `StepMath.CheckedAdd` and `StepMath.TrySum`
* CI: engine-watch's `baseline_ref` in `protocol-watch.yml` is `3.4.1`
* Docs: the Amounts guide (`DocFx/Amounts-Guide.md`, `Amounts-Guide.ru.md`) and the summaries of `OfferCreateCrossing`, `PaymentFlow`, `Pathfinder` and `PathFinding` name rippled 3.4.1 as the release the engine follows
* Tests: `TestUCheckedStepAdd` replays the `checkedAdd` assertions of `MathUtilities.h` on MPT and XRP amounts and checks the step and flow sums
## 11.13.0.0 11/10/2026

* **Breaking:** `Blob.FromJson` (`Xrpl.BinaryCodec` 11.13.0.0) refuses a value that is not an even-length hex string, as rippled and xrpl.js do; it encoded such a value as ASCII, with each non-ASCII character becoming `?`. This covers every Blob field - `MemoData`, `MemoType`, `MemoFormat`, `Domain`, `URI`, `Data`, `DIDDocument`, `MPTokenMetadata` and the rest. Signing raises `InvalidJsonException` naming the field. An empty string is still zero bytes. Migration: pass text through `ConvertStringToHex()`, or `Blob.FromAscii` / `Blob.FromString` when building a `Blob` directly (#271)
* **Breaking:** the Oracle `Provider`, `AssetClass` and `URI` properties of `OracleSet`, `OracleSetResponse`, `IOracleSet` and `LOOracle` are `OracleBlob` instead of `string` (#272). `OracleBlob` holds the field's bytes and writes back exactly the bytes it read; the string form could not tell the text `C328FF` from the bytes C3 28 FF and signed the hex digits as text. Members: `FromText`, `FromHex`, `FromBytes`, `Bytes`, `Hex`, `IsText`, `TryGetText`, `ToArray`, byte equality, and `ToString()` returning `Hex`. Migration: assigning a string still compiles and is taken as text (`Provider = "chainlink"`); bytes given as hex go through `OracleBlob.FromHex`; reading text is `TryGetText(out string text)`, which declines bytes that are not printable ASCII
* `OracleBlobConverter` replaces `OracleHexStringConverter`. Reading refuses a value that is not hex (`JsonException`) and keeps any bytes the node sends
* Tests: `TestUBlobHexOnly`; `TestUOracleBlobConverter` replaces `TestUOracleHexStringConverter`; `TestVerify_OracleSet_Serialization_MultiplePriceData` feeds the codec hex; `TestIOracle` compares the read-back fields as bytes; `BookCrossingHarness` and `TestIPermissionedDex` give `CredentialType` as hex

## 11.12.0.0 05/10/2026

* **The protocol schema follows the nightly stand's pin, rippled develop at `0229c29`** (`Xrpl.BinaryCodec` 11.12.0.0): the SmartEscrow fields `GasLimit`, `BytecodeSizeLimit`, `GasPrice`, `Gas`, `GasUsed` (UInt32 81-85), `Bytecode` (Blob 47) and `VMReturnCode` (Int32 3), and the results `temINVALID_BYTECODE`, `temTEMP_DISABLED`, `tefNO_BYTECODE`, `tefBYTECODE_NOT_INCLUDED`, `tecOUT_OF_GAS` and `tecBYTECODE_REJECTED`, taken from the node's `server_definitions`
* `LedgerRules.FromNodeAsync` sets `LargeNumbers` when `MPTokensV2` is enabled, as rippled develop's `setCurrentTransactionRules` now selects the large `Number` mantissa for it
* `TestULedgerRules` checks the scale `LedgerRules.FromFeatures` gives for each amendment that selects the large mantissa, and without them
* **Reliable submission reports what happened to a transaction, not when the SDK stopped looking** (#266). `SubmitAndWait` and `SubmitRequestAndWait`:
  * decide expiry against the validated ledger instead of the open one
  * look the transaction up with `min_ledger` (the ledger validated before submission) and `max_ledger` (`LastLedgerSequence`), and report it expired only when the node's `searched_all` proves it in none of them, or, since rippled answers `searched_all` only for ranges where every ledger holds a transaction, when each ledger of the range, read validated with its transactions once `LastLedgerSequence` is validated, does not list it (up to 256 ledgers)
  * report a transaction that is in none of the range as unknown, not expired, when the submission answered `tefPAST_SEQ`, `tefNO_TICKET` or `tefALREADY`: the blob or its sequence was already used, possibly by an earlier submission applied before the range
  * give up as unknown when the server's validated ledger stays where it is for 60 polls
  * end the wait on a provisional result only for a `tem`. Without `failHard` a `tec`, `ter`, `tef` or `tel` is waited for, a `tec` reported once validated with its metadata. Under `failHard` any other result but `tefALREADY`, `tefPAST_SEQ` and `tefNO_TICKET` is looked up once and followed if found, and otherwise fails with `ReachedLedger` false. A blob submitted again after it was applied returns its success
* `TransactionExpiredException`: never applied, with `Hash`, `LastLedgerSequence`, `MinLedger`, `PreliminaryResult` and `SearchedAll` (proven by the lookup's own `searched_all` rather than by reading the ledgers)
* `TransactionOutcomeUnknownException`: possibly applied, with the same fields and the cause in `InnerException`. Raised when the submit request fails other than by an error answer from the node, or with `internalSubmit` or `internalJson`, when a lookup or a ledger read fails while waiting, and when the validated ledger is five past `LastLedgerSequence` while the node lacks part of the range
* `TransactionWaitCanceledException`, an `OperationCanceledException` with the caller's token, raised when the caller cancels after the submit request was sent; its `Hash`, `LastLedgerSequence`, `MinLedger` and `PreliminaryResult` resume the wait
* `SubmitSugar.WaitForTransactionOutcome(hash, lastLedgerSequence, minLedger)` waits for a transaction already submitted, without submitting it again; it accepts a `minLedger` past `lastLedgerSequence`, as an unknown outcome can carry
* `ErrorResponse.SearchedAll`
* `TransactionFailedException(message, engineResult, hash, reachedLedger)` states `ReachedLedger` where the code alone does not tell
* **Breaking:** an expired transaction raised `ValidationException`, the type that also means nothing was submitted; it now raises `TransactionExpiredException`. A failure while waiting raised `ValidationException`, `RippledException` or `XrplException`; it now raises `TransactionOutcomeUnknownException`. A disconnect, a timeout or a not-connected failure of the submit request, which propagated as it was, is now `TransactionOutcomeUnknownException` around it. A provisional `tec`, `ter`, `tef` or `tel` no longer fails at once without `failHard`: it fails, or expires, once `LastLedgerSequence` is validated. Migration: catch `TransactionExpiredException` where an expiry was caught as `ValidationException`; on `TransactionOutcomeUnknownException`, call `WaitForTransactionOutcome` with its `Hash`, `LastLedgerSequence` and `MinLedger` before submitting a replacement
* **Behaviour change:** `GetLedgerIndex` returns the validated ledger, as its documentation and xrpl.js's `getLedgerIndex` say; it returned the open one. Autofill's `LastLedgerSequence` becomes validated + 20, one ledger shorter
* `TestUSubmitOutcome` covers each provisional result with and without `failHard`, the expiry by `searched_all` and by the ledgers, a held transaction still cached past its last ledger, a ledger that lists the transaction before the lookup returns it, a ledger the server lacks, failures while waiting, and which submit failures left nothing on the network; `TestIReliableSubmission` expires a `terPRE_SEQ` payment, submits an applied blob again at once and ledgers later, and tracks a transaction already sent, on a node
* `MainnetNode-Guide` (both languages) says the expiry needs the node to hold the ledgers from submission to `LastLedgerSequence`

## Xrpl.X402 1.0.1 / Xrpl.X402.AspNetCore 1.0.1 05/10/2026

* `XrplWalletX402Signer` counts a payment's validity window from the open ledger, validated + 1
* `LedgerSettlingFacilitator` waits once more, with `WaitForTransactionOutcome`, for a settlement whose outcome the client could not tell, and serves the resource if it validated. One that stays unknown is refused as `settlement_unknown`, with the transaction hash, network and payer in the response, which `RequirePayment` returns as `PAYMENT-RESPONSE` beside the 402 challenge; a final failure or an expiry is `settlement_failed`. All three were `settlement_error`
* `PaymentResponseEnvelope` documents `transaction`, `network` and `payer` on `settlement_unknown`
* `LedgerSettlingFacilitatorTests` covers each outcome, `RequirePaymentTests` the receipt beside a `settlement_unknown` challenge; `X402-Guide` (both languages) and the `Xrpl.X402.AspNetCore` README describe `settlement_unknown`

## 11.11.0.0 30/09/2026

* The engine reads `fixAMMv1_1` (`swapAssetIn`, `swapAssetOut`), `fixAMMv1_3` (an offer's `checkInvariant`) and `fixReducedOffersV2` (`limitIn`) as the ledger has them inside every transaction, whatever `useRulesGuards` decides; they read as disabled only outside a transaction (#250)
* `DexSnapshot.FromNodeAsync` reads the domain of every domain or hybrid offer it loads, and the credentials of the offer owners that domain accepts, also for an order or a payment without a `DomainID`
* **`AmmOfferMath`: an AMM pool's offer arithmetic** (#253, `Xrpl.Amounts`): `SwapIn` (`swapAssetIn`), `SwapOut` (`swapAssetOut`), `ChangeSpotPriceQuality` and `PoolOffer` - the offer a pool puts beside an order book on one strand (`AMMLiquidity::getOffer`), as an `AmmPoolOffer` with its `In`, `Out` and `Quality`. The engine generates its pool offers through the same code
* `XrplQuality.Round(digits)` (`Quality::round`) and `OfferCreateCrossing.RoundToTickSize(takerPays, takerGets, tickSize, sell, rules)`, the amounts an offer is written with under a tick size (#253)
* `DexSnapshotOptions.Ledger` reads a snapshot at a given ledger, and `DexSnapshot.LedgerSequence` records the ledger a snapshot was read at (#252). `QuotePaymentAsync` and `QuoteOfferCreateAsync` make every deeper read at the ledger of the first
* `DexSnapshotOptions.OwnerFundsFromBook` builds offer owners from `book_offers`' `owner_funds` instead of reading their accounts and trust lines, listing them in `DexSnapshot.ApproximatedOwners`; `DexSnapshot.UnlimitedTaker` quotes an `OfferCreate` or a `Payment` for a taker without a wallet, with unlimited funds (#252)
* **How the engine took the liquidity** (#251, `Xrpl.Amounts`): `OfferCrossingResult` and `PaymentFlowResult` gain `Fills` - each offer and pool slice taken, in order, as an `OfferFill` with its `Pass`, `Strand`, `Step`, `OfferIndex` or pool `Owner`, `In`, `Out` and `Quality` - and `Passes`, each strand tried in each pass as a `FlowPass` with its `FlowPassOutcome` (`Taken`, `Dry` - also for a strand with no liquidity at all - `BelowLimitQuality`, `OutOfReach`). `OfferChange` gains `Reason` (`OfferRemovalReason`: `Consumed`, `Unfunded`, `Expired`, `Empty`, `DeepFrozen`, `NotInDomain`, `TooSmall`, `Unauthorized`, `SelfCrossed`, `Cancelled`) and `FilledTakerPays` / `FilledTakerGets`
* `AmmOfferMath.MaxTradingFee` (1000, 1%); `AmmOfferMath` and a snapshot's `DexAmmPool.TradingFee` / `DiscountedFee` refuse a higher fee with `ArgumentException` (#257)
* `LedgerRules.FromNodeAsync` and `Amounts-Guide` (both languages) say that the rules come from the ledger's `Amendments` object, which amendments a node applies from its config's `[features]` stanza are not in (#256)
* `GetBalanceChanges` and `Amounts-Guide` (both languages) describe the `getText` form of `Currency.Value` (#254); `Amounts-Guide` also covers `AmmOfferMath`, `RoundToTickSize`, `DexSnapshotOptions.Ledger` and `OwnerFundsFromBook`, `UnlimitedTaker`, the fills, the passes and the removal reasons
* `TestUDexGaps` checks the removal reasons - expired, unfunded, consumed, unauthorized, cancelled, out of the domain, self-crossed - and the order of the fills, and `TestUPaymentFlow` a pass below the limit quality and two strands dropped as out of reach before any is run. The integration harness requires every offer's fills, replayed from its amounts in the snapshot, to leave what the metadata's final fields show, the fills to add up to `FilledTakerPays` / `FilledTakerGets`, and each pool's fills, replayed in order, to give its balance on the node
* `TestIBookCrossing.OwnerFundsFromTheBookCrossAsTheNodeDoes` crosses an underfunded maker and a transfer fee from a snapshot built from `owner_funds` and compares it with the metadata; `UnlimitedTakerQuotesAsAFundedOne` compares the unlimited taker's offer and payment quotes with a funded account's
* `TestUAmmOfferMath.TradingFeeAboveTheProtocolMaximumIsRefused`
* `TestUAmmOfferMath` replays `Offer_test.cpp`'s `testTickSize` offers through `RoundToTickSize` and covers `XrplQuality.Round` and the pool offers; `TestIBookCrossing.SnapshotReadsTheLedgerAsked` reads a book before and after an offer is placed
* `TestUOfferCreateCrossing.CurrentRulesAreSetInsideEveryTransaction`; `TestUXrplAmount` pins the `getText` boundaries and reads the scientific form through `ValueAsNumber`
* The integration harness waits for a ledger closed after an expiration for up to five minutes of wall time

## 11.10.0.0 27/09/2026

* **`XrplAmount`: an amount over the ledger's whole range** (#150, `Xrpl.Amounts`). It holds XRP drops, MPT units or an issued currency as a 16-digit mantissa with an exponent from -96 to 80, as rippled's `STAmount` does. `Parse`, `TryParse`, `FromNumber` (`STAmount::fromNumber`), `FromCurrency` / `ToCurrency`, `ToString` (`STAmount::getText`), comparison and `KindOf`. `AmountKind` (`Xrp`, `Iou`, `Mpt`) moves from `Xrpl.Sugar` to `Xrpl.Amounts` and becomes public
  * `Currency.ToXrplAmount()` reads a wire amount exactly, beyond what `ValueAsNumber` holds
* **`XrplAmountMath`: `STAmount` arithmetic** (#238): `Add`, `Subtract`, `Multiply(amount, fraction, rounding)` (`multiply(amount, frac, rm)`), `Multiply(v1, v2, asset)`, `Divide`, and the directed rounding of offer crossing - `MulRound`, `MulRoundStrict`, `DivRound`, `DivRoundStrict` - with `canonicalizeRound` / `canonicalizeRoundStrict`, the `MPTokensV2` `Number` path, and the fixed-width behaviour of `STAmount`'s constructor (a mantissa above `int64` wraps for an issued currency and loses its last digit for XRP and MPT)
* **`XrplQuality`: rippled's `Quality`** (#238): the 64-bit encoding, `FromAmounts` (`getRate`), `FromBookDirectory`, `Rate`, rippled's ordering, `CeilIn`, `CeilOut`, `CeilInStrict`, `CeilOutStrict` and `WithinRelativeDistance`
  * `Offer.BookQuality` is the quality the node crosses an offer at, from its `BookDirectory`; `Offer.RemainingQuality` the exact ratio of what it has left
* **`OfferCrossing`: one offer sized as `BookStep` sizes it** (#238): `Fund` (transfer fees and the owner's funds), `LimitStepIn`, `LimitStepOut`, `ForwardPass` (the forward pass settling on the reverse pass's output), `Cross`, `LimitIn` / `LimitOut` (`TOffer::limitIn` / `limitOut`), `MulRatio` for XRP, MPT and issued currencies, and `PassesQualityLimit`; the result is an `OfferStep`
  * `LedgerRules.FixReducedOffersV2` selects `ceilInStrict` in `LimitIn`; `FromNodeAsync` reads it
* **`OfferCreateCrossing`: an `OfferCreate` crossed over whole order books and AMM pools** (`Xrpl.Amounts`). `Cross` repeats rippled 3.4.0's `flowCross` against a `DexSnapshot`: the direct book and, for two issued currencies, the bridge through XRP; every offer of a quality level per pass, in book order, with expired, empty, deep-frozen, unfunded and too-small offers removed; the owner's funds after what the transaction already moved (`PaymentSandbox`'s deferred credits); the AMM pool's synthetic offer - sized to the book's quality on a single strand (`changeSpotPriceQuality`, `maxOffer`) and in Fibonacci slices on two; the `StrandFlow` loop with `limitOut`; `tfPassive`, `tfSell`, `tfImmediateOrCancel`, `tfFillOrKill`; tick size; the remainder and the reserve for placing it
  * `OfferCrossingResult`: `EngineResult`, `Paid`, `Received`, `PlacedTakerPays` / `PlacedTakerGets`, `Offers` (`OfferChange`), `Pools` (`AmmPoolChange`) and `BalanceChanges` (`BalanceChange`), the fee included
  * `DexSnapshot` with `DexAccount`, `DexTrustLine`, `DexOffer` and `DexAmmPool`; `DexSnapshot.FromNodeAsync` reads the books, pools, accounts and trust lines a crossing reaches at one validated ledger
  * `LedgerRules.FixAMMv1_2` and `LedgerRules.FixFillOrKill`; `FromNodeAsync` reads them
* **`PaymentFlow`: a `Payment` evaluated through the payment engine** (#240, `Xrpl.Amounts`). `Evaluate` repeats rippled 3.4.0's `Payment` - the `tem` codes of `preflight`, the destination checks of `preclaim`, deposit authorization - and `RippleCalc` against a `DexSnapshot`: `toStrands`, with the implied source, SendMax issuer, book and destination issuer and its path and loop checks; direct steps with `QualityIn` / `QualityOut`, trust limits, NoRipple, freezes, authorization and the transfer fee of each issuer the payment ripples through; book steps that leave the output fee to the next step; SendMax, DeliverMin, `tfPartialPayment`, `tfLimitQuality` and `tfNoDirectRipple`; and a direct XRP payment against the reserve
  * `PaymentFlowResult`: `EngineResult`, `Applied`, `DeliveredAmount`, `Paid`, `Offers`, `Pools` and `BalanceChanges`
  * `DexSnapshot.FromNodeAsync(client, payment)` reads the trust lines along the payment's paths and default path, the books and pools on them, the accounts, and the destination's deposit preauthorizations
  * `DexTrustLine` describes both sides of a line: `Limit` / `PeerLimit`, `QualityIn` / `QualityOut` / `PeerQualityIn` / `PeerQualityOut`, `NoRipple` / `PeerNoRipple`, `FrozenByAccount`, `Authorized` / `PeerAuthorized`. `DexAccount` gains `RequireAuth`, `RequireDestinationTag`, `DepositAuth` and `DepositPreauthorized`
  * `OfferCreateCrossing` builds its strands through the same `toStrands`, and its `BalanceChanges` list both sides of every trust line, as the metadata does
* **The engine's checks around the flow** (`Xrpl.Amounts`):
  * `OfferCreateCrossing.Cross(snapshot, offer, rules)` takes the SDK's `OfferCreate` and repeats its `preflight` (`temINVALID_FLAG`, `temMALFORMED`, `temBAD_EXPIRATION`, `temBAD_SEQUENCE`, `temBAD_OFFER`, `temREDUNDANT`, `temBAD_CURRENCY`) and `preclaim` (`tecFROZEN`, `tecUNFUNDED_OFFER`, `temBAD_SEQUENCE`, `tecEXPIRED`, `checkAcceptAsset`'s `tecNO_ISSUER` / `tecNO_LINE` / `tecNO_AUTH` / `tecFROZEN`), cancels the offer `OfferSequence` names before crossing, and refuses an expired offer. A `tec` result other than `tecKILLED` keeps only the fee
  * `OfferCrossingResult` gains `Applied`, `TrustLines` and `NeedsDeeperBooks`; `PaymentFlowResult` gains `TrustLines` and `NeedsDeeperBooks`. `TrustLineChange` names a line created or deleted
  * Book steps remove the offer of an owner the issuer does not authorize to hold what the offer asks for (`requireAuth`), before its quality is checked
  * Trust lines follow `trustCreate` and `directSendNoFeeIOU`: a line created on the way in is owned by its receiver, with NoRipple on each side as the account's `DefaultRipple` implies; a sender whose positive balance is gone and whose side is at its defaults stops paying the line's reserve, and the line is deleted when the receiver does not pay it either. `DexTrustLine` gains `Reserve` and `PeerReserve`; `DexAccount` gains `Sequence`, `DefaultRipple` and `DisallowIncomingTrustline`
  * Permissioned DEX: an `OfferCreate` or a `Payment` with a `DomainID` walks the domain's books - its offers and the hybrid ones, no AMM pool - with members only, refusing outsiders with `tecNO_PERMISSION` and expired members with `tecEXPIRED`; a domain offer whose owner left the domain is removed. `DexSnapshot` gains `Domains` and `Credentials` (`DexDomain`, `DexCredential`, `DexCredentialType`); `DexOffer` gains `DomainId` and `Hybrid`
  * Credentials: a payment's `CredentialIDs` are checked (`temMALFORMED`, `tecBAD_CREDENTIALS`, `tecEXPIRED`) and meet deposit authorization by the credential sets `DexAccount.DepositPreauthorizedCredentials` lists
  * Books read in part: `DexSnapshot.PartialBooks` (`DexBook`) lists the books a snapshot holds only the top of, and a result that walked past what was read says so in `NeedsDeeperBooks`. `DexSnapshotOptions.BookDepth` reads a book by walking its directories - quality by quality, page by page, unfunded offers included - instead of one `book_offers` page, which a public node caps at 100 offers
  * `DexSnapshot.FromNodeAsync` takes a `DexSnapshotOptions`, and an `OfferCreate` in a new overload; it reads the domain, its books, the credentials the domain accepts for the accounts involved, those a payment presents, and the offer `OfferSequence` cancels
  * `BookOffersRequest.Domain` and the book offer's `DomainID`
* **`DexQuoteSugar`: quotes against a node's ledger** (`Xrpl.Sugar`). `QuotePaymentAsync` and `QuoteOfferCreateAsync` read a snapshot and run the engine, reading deeper while the result `NeedsDeeperBooks`. `QuoteDeliverAsync` quotes the cost of delivering an exact amount and `QuoteSpendAsync` the delivery an exact spend buys, along the paths `ripple_path_find` returns; each returns a `PaymentQuote` with the `Payment` to sign
* **`PathFinding`: payment paths found locally, as `ripple_path_find` finds them** (`Xrpl.Amounts`). `FindAsync` repeats rippled 3.4.0's `PathRequest` and `Pathfinder`: the source assets (`source_currencies`, SendMax, or every asset the source holds), the path-shape table of each payment type up to the search level, `addLink` over trust lines and order books with `getPathsOut`'s candidate ranking and the ledger-sequence tie-break, every complete path ranked through the payment engine (`getPathLiquidity`, `rankPaths`), `getBestPaths` with the covering full-liquidity path, and the cost of the chosen paths; `convert_all` with a destination amount of `-1`
  * `PathFindRequest`, `PathFindResult` (`Alternatives`, `DestinationCurrencies`, `DestinationTagRequired`, the node's error codes) and `PathFindAlternative` (`SourceAmount`, `PathsComputed`, `DestinationAmount`)
  * `PathfindingSource.FromSnapshot` and `PathfindingSource.FromNodeAsync`: the ledger a search reads - accounts and trust lines as the search reaches them, in owner-directory order with `AssetCache`'s incoming/outgoing sets, and the state for ranking read along the paths found, at one ledger
  * `IBookIndex` and `BookIndex`: the order books, which rippled's `OrderBookDB` holds and a client supplies - `FromLedgerAsync` (every book directory and AMM pool of a ledger), `FromAssetsAsync` (the books between given assets, at most `MaxProbeAssets`), `FromAccountsAsync` (between the assets the accounts' trust lines hold, owe or trust for), `FromSnapshot`, and `Observe` for the books later transactions create
  * `DexQuoteSugar.FindPathsAsync`, and a `books` argument on `QuoteDeliverAsync` and `QuoteSpendAsync` that takes the paths from the local search instead of `ripple_path_find`
  * `RipplePathFindRequest.Domain`
* **The engine outside a transaction**: rippled reads some amendments from the current transaction's rules, which a node has none of outside a transaction - in `ripple_path_find`. `swapAssetIn` and `swapAssetOut` (`fixAMMv1_1`), an offer's `checkInvariant` (`fixAMMv1_3`) and `limitIn` (`fixReducedOffersV2`) now read them that way, while `BookStep`, `AMMLiquidity` and `changeSpotPriceQuality` keep reading the view's rules; outside a transaction `Number` keeps its large scale
* The engine no longer moves XRP straight from one account to another along a strand of two XRP endpoints (`isDirectXrpToXrp`), as rippled's flow refuses
* **`GetBalanceChanges` computes issued-currency deltas as `XrplAmount`** (#154): the difference is exact, then rounded once to 16 digits to nearest with ties to even, and written as rippled writes it. A balance beyond `decimal` is reported instead of throwing `AmountOutOfRangeException`
  * **Behaviour change:** `Currency.Value` of an issued-currency delta is the `STAmount::getText` form, in scientific notation when the exponent is above -5 or below -25 (from about 1e11 up and below about 1e-10): 123456789012 is written `1234567890120000e-4`, where 11.9.0.0 wrote `123456789012`. Read it with `ToXrplAmount()` or `ValueAsNumber`, not as display text or with `NumberStyles.Number`
* **Breaking: `Xrpl.Utils.Quality` is removed.** It was an empty class; the quality of an offer is `XrplQuality`
* `Amounts-Guide` (both languages) covers `XrplAmount`, the arithmetic, `XrplQuality`, `OfferCrossing`, `OfferCreateCrossing`, `PaymentFlow`, permissioned domains and credentials, reading books from a node, quotes, and finding paths
* `protocol-watch.yml`: `engine-watch` also follows the path finder's sources (`Pathfinder`, `PathRequest`, `AssetCache`, `TrustLine`, `AccountAssets`, `OrderBookDBImpl`, `RipplePathFind`, `STPathSet`) and `Rules.cpp`; a path added to its list starts from the baseline ref
* The Blazor demo has a Paths & quotes page: quotes to deliver or spend exactly, and path searches compared with the node's
* `TestUAmountVectors` replays 8,808 results computed by rippled 3.4.0's `STAmount`, `Quality` and `mulRatio` code over every `Number` scale and rounding mode; `Tests/Xrpl.Tests/Fixtures/Amount` holds the vectors and the generator. `TestUXrplAmount` pins the `ceilIn` / `ceilOut` cases of `Quality_test.cpp`, the `getRate` cases of `STAmount_test.cpp` and the text form. `TestIOfferCrossing` crosses offers on a node - partially, with a transfer fee, from an underfunded maker, between two issued currencies - and compares every amount with the metadata, and reads a 5e90 balance change
* `TestUOfferCreateCrossing` rebuilds the offer crossings of rippled's `AMM_test.cpp` (`testBasicPaymentEngine`) as snapshots and requires the same pool balances, remaining offers and holdings under each amendment set, and replays `testFixChangeSpotPriceQuality`. `TestIBookCrossing`, `TestIBookCrossingBridge` and `TestIBookCrossingAmm` build books and pools on a node, cross them locally and on the node, and compare every offer, pool and balance the transaction changed
* `TestUPaymentFlow` replays the payments of rippled's `Flow_test.cpp` (`testDirectStep`, `testLineQuality`) as snapshots, and `TestUStrandBuilder` the strands of `PayStrand_test.cpp` (`testToStrand`). `TestIPaymentFlow` sends payments on a node - rippling through intermediaries with qualities and transfer fees, through books and pools, on two strands, with DeliverMin and the limit quality, along paths from `ripple_path_find`, and the ones the node refuses - and compares each with the metadata
* `TestUDexGaps` covers the offer's own checks, `OfferSequence`, `Offer_test.cpp`'s `testMissingAuth`, trust lines created and deleted, domain and hybrid books, credentials and deposit authorization, partial books, and the book directory keys against mainnet's. `TestIOfferCreateChecks`, `TestIPermissionedDex` and `TestIDexQuotes` run the same on a node - an unauthorized owner's offer, frozen, unfunded, expired and malformed offers, `OfferSequence`, lines created and deleted, domain and hybrid offers, members and outsiders, expired credentials, credentials meeting deposit authorization, a 111-offer book read by walking its directories, and quotes submitted as quoted - and compare each with the metadata, trust lines created and deleted included
* `TestUPathFinding` replays `Path_test.cpp`'s searches as snapshots, with jtx's accounts - direct, through a gateway, rippling, consume-all, through an offer, the four best of many paths, the negative cases - and `TestIPathFinding` runs searches locally and through `ripple_path_find` at the same ledger - through gateways, intermediaries, books and pools, across currencies, consume-all, source-issuer selection, every source asset, a domain book - and requires the same alternatives, and pays one as found
* `.ci-config/rippled.cfg` and `rippled.batchv11.cfg` store ledgers in NuDB instead of RocksDB: standalone xrpld with RocksDB never finishes closing a ledger of more than about 100 transactions, and building a deep book beside the other classes reaches that

## 11.9.0.0 26/09/2026

* **`XrplNumber` is the value type of the XRPL `Number` fields** (#214, part of #211). It lives in `Xrpl.BinaryCodec.Numbers` and holds a value the way rippled's `Number` class does on the large mantissa scale: a sign, a mantissa in [10^18, 10^19 - 1] and an exponent in [-32768, 32768].
  * `Parse` / `TryParse` read decimal and scientific text; `ToString()` writes what rippled's `to_string(Number)` writes, `1e13` for 10^13; `Mantissa` / `Exponent` give the wire pair of `Number::mantissa()` / `Number::exponent()`
  * equality and ordering by value; implicit conversion from `int`, explicit from `long` and `decimal`, explicit to `decimal` and `TryToDecimal`, which rounds to nearest (ties to even) past 28 fractional digits and fails on overflow
  * `XrplNumberJsonConverter` is applied to the type itself: reads a JSON string or number, writes the string
  * `TestUXrplNumber` carries the `to_string` vectors of rippled's `NumberTest.to_string` and the text vectors of xrpl.js `st-number.test.ts`; `TestUNumberFields` checks model JSON and the encoded bytes
* **A `Number` value the ledger cannot hold exactly is refused instead of rounded.** `NumberType.FromString` rounded `9223372036854775895` to `9223372036854775900` and signed that; it now throws `FormatException`, as rippled's `numberFromJson` refuses the value with "number cannot be represented". The limit is 19 significant digits, and 18 above 9223372036854775807
* **`NumberType` is built on `XrplNumber`.** `NumberType.Value` returns it and `NumberType(XrplNumber)` creates the canonical wire pair. `ToJson()` and `ToString()` now write rippled's text, so the codec decodes `PrincipalRequested` 10000000000000 as `"1e13"`, not `"10000000000000"`. `FromParser` accepts the wire exponent 32769 that rippled produces for a mantissa above int64 (`9223372036854775810e32768`), and refuses a mantissa of `long.MinValue`
* **Breaking:** the Number fields of the vault and lending models are `XrplNumber?` instead of `string`:
  * `LOVault`: `AssetsTotal`, `AssetsAvailable`, `AssetsMaximum`, `LossUnrealized`
  * `LOLoanBroker`: `DebtTotal`, `DebtMaximum`, `CoverAvailable`
  * `LOLoan`: `PrincipalOutstanding`, `TotalValueOutstanding`, `PeriodicPayment`, `ManagementFeeOutstanding`, `LoanOriginationFee`, `LoanServiceFee`, `LatePaymentFee`, `ClosePaymentFee`
  * `ILoanSet` / `LoanSet` / `LoanSetResponse`: `PrincipalRequested`, `LoanOriginationFee`, `LoanServiceFee`, `LatePaymentFee`, `ClosePaymentFee`
  * `ILoanBrokerSet` / `LoanBrokerSet` / `LoanBrokerSetResponse`: `DebtMaximum`
  * `IVaultCreate` / `VaultCreate` / `VaultCreateResponse` and `IVaultSet` / `VaultSet` / `VaultSetResponse`: `AssetsMaximum`

  Migration: add `using Xrpl.BinaryCodec.Numbers;`. Assign an `int` directly (`PrincipalRequested = 10000000`), anything else through `XrplNumber.Parse("...")` or a cast from `long` or `decimal`. Read values with `ToString()`, which is rippled's text, compare them with `==` and `<`, and convert with `TryToDecimal` or `(decimal)`. Code that parsed the string with `decimal.Parse` must handle the scientific form or switch to `TryToDecimal`
* **An unreadable Number costs one field, not the object.** `LenientXrplNumberConverter` is applied to the Number fields of `LOVault`, `LOLoanBroker`, `LOLoan` and the four `*Response` models above: a value this version refuses reads as `null` and the rest of the entry, an `account_objects` page or a transaction still deserializes.
* `XrplNumber.Parse` works in time linear in the length of the text: it accumulates at most 19 significant digits and carries leading and trailing zeros as an exponent (`Parse_StaysLinearOnMegabyteInput`). The written exponent may reach ±10^9; whether the value fits is decided after the fraction digits are counted, so `"0e100001"` is zero
* `new NumberType(0, exponent)` with an exponent other than `int.MinValue` or 0 throws `ArgumentOutOfRangeException`; `FromParser` already refused those bytes and reports them as `FormatException`
* `Autofill` reads `LOLoan.PeriodicPayment` and `LoanServiceFee` through `TryToDecimal` when it estimates the `LoanPay` fee
* `LendingProtocol-Guide` and `Vault-Guide` (both languages) assign Number fields as numbers, and the lending guide describes `XrplNumber`
* **Previews through `simulate`** (#215, part of #211). `PreviewSugar` adds `PreviewLoanPay`, `PreviewVaultDeposit`, `PreviewVaultWithdraw`, `PreviewAMMDeposit`, `PreviewAMMWithdraw` and `PreviewBalanceChanges` to `IXrplClient`. Each autofills the transaction, runs it through `simulate` and returns a `TransactionPreview<TOutcome>`: `EngineResult`, `WouldSucceed`, the simulated `Metadata`, the autofilled `Transaction`, and an `Outcome` read from the metadata.
  * `LoanPaymentOutcome` gives `PaymentsMade`, `PrincipalPaid`, `PaidToVault`, `InterestToVault`, `PaidToBroker`, `TotalPaid` and `LoanAfter` of a `LoanPay`. The split follows the money: the vault receives principal and interest, and the broker receives the rest
  * `VaultOutcome` gives `AccountAssetChange`, `AccountShareChange`, `VaultAssetChange` and `VaultAfter` of a vault deposit or withdrawal
  * `AmmOutcome` gives `AssetChange`, `Asset2Change`, `LpTokenChange` and `AmmAfter` of an AMM deposit or withdrawal
  * each outcome also has a `FromMetadata`, which reads the same figures from a validated transaction
  * for an account that issues the asset, the change sums every trust line in that currency
  * `VaultOutcome.FromMetadata` reads the vault named by the transaction's `VaultID` and refuses a transaction other than `VaultDeposit`, `VaultWithdraw` or `VaultClawback`
  * `TestIPreviewLoanVault` and `TestIPreviewAmm` preview a transaction, submit the previewed transaction unchanged, and compare the two outcomes field by field: an XRP `LoanPay`, an MPT `LoanPay`, a vault deposit and withdrawal, and a single-sided AMM deposit and withdrawal
* **`PreviewLoanSet` shows a loan's terms before the borrower signs** (#217, part of #211). It simulates the `LoanSet` with an empty `CounterpartySignature`, which `simulate` accepts unverified, and returns a `LoanSetOutcome`: `LoanId`, the created `Loan`, `BorrowerReceives`, `PaidFromVault`, `InterestTotal`, `ServiceFeesTotal` and `TotalToRepay`. `LoanSetOutcome.FromMetadata` reads the same from a validated `LoanSet`
  * `TestIPreviewLoanVault.TestPreviewLoanSet_MatchesTheLoanTheSignedTransactionCreates` previews a loan unsigned, creates it with the borrower's signature, and compares the two loans field by field
* **`LoanSchedule.Project` projects a loan's regular payments** (#217, #220, part of #211). It starts from a `Loan` entry - from the ledger or from `PreviewLoanSet` - and repeats rippled's `computePaymentComponents` for each payment in rippled's own arithmetic - `XrplNumber` in the `NumberContext` the amendments select, with `roundToAsset` computed as `STAmount` computes it, 16 significant digits and then the loan's scale for an issued currency: the step from the rounded state to the rounded theoretical state after it, capped at the rounded `PeriodicPayment`, with the final payment clearing the rest. Each `LoanScheduleRow` has `DueDate`, `Principal`, `Interest`, `ManagementFee`, `ServiceFee`, `Total` and the outstanding amounts after it
  * `LedgerRules.FixCleanup3_2_0` selects the rounding of the target state and `FixCleanup3_3_0` the `Number` scale; `LedgerRules.FromNodeAsync` reads both from the node
  * `TestIPreviewLoanVault.TestLoanSchedule_MatchesEveryPaymentTheNodeTakes` (XRP, 6 payments, 10% management fee) and `TestLoanSchedule_Mpt_MatchesEveryPaymentTheNodeTakes` (MPT, 5 payments) and `TestLoanSchedule_Iou_MatchesEveryPaymentTheNodeTakes` (USD: 7, 3 and 12 payments with fractional principals, management fees and service fees) project a loan, pay every payment and compare each with its row; `TestULoanSchedule` pins the recorded splits
* The Blazor test client (`Tests/TestsClients/Blazor-WebAssembly`) has a **Loan preview** tab next to the connection page. It shows a new loan's terms from `PreviewLoanSet` and its schedule from `LoanSchedule.Project`, or the remaining schedule of an existing loan, and checks the terms against `LoanSet`'s preflight limits first. On a test network its **Full flow** mode runs the whole cycle: it funds a broker and a borrower, creates a vault and broker, previews the terms, co-signs and submits the `LoanSet`, and pays each payment next to its projected row
* The Blazor test client has an **AMM preview** tab (`Pages/AmmPreview.razor`, #225). It loads a pool with `amm_info` - an account's LP tokens and its trading fee, the auction slot's discount included, when an account is given - and quotes any mode `AmmLiquidity` covers next to the node's `simulate` answer, with the pool the ledger will hold afterwards
* The Blazor test client has a **Vault preview** tab (`Pages/VaultPreview.razor`) that quotes `VaultShares` next to `simulate`, the phase of a closed-ended vault judged at the validated close time. The **Loan preview** tab shows `LoanOrigination.Compute` next to the node's `LoanSet`, and previews a payment on an existing loan - `LoanPayments.PaymentForAmount` with or without `tfLoanOverpayment`, `LatePaymentDue`, `FullPaymentDue` - next to `simulate`; its full flow can create a loan that allows overpayments. The **AMM preview** tab gains the effective-price and empty-pool modes, and its asset selector binds to a string
* `LendingProtocol-Guide` (both languages) shows `PreviewLoanSet` and `LoanSchedule`
* `ServerFeatures.GetByName` returns null for a name the node does not list; it returned an empty pair whose `Value` was null (`GetByName_UnknownName_IsNull`)
* **`LoanPayments`** reads a `Loan` entry without asking the node:
  * `RegularPaymentCap`: `PeriodicPayment` rounded up to the asset, plus `LoanServiceFee`; on the final payment, `TotalValueOutstanding` plus `LoanServiceFee`. Each part is rounded up on the `XrplNumber` before it becomes a `decimal`, with a step finer than 10^-28 rounded up to 10^-28; a cap beyond `decimal` returns null
  * `IsPaymentLate` and `IsPastGracePeriod`, with an inclusive or exclusive boundary, comparing in UTC: a local time is converted, and an unspecified one is taken as UTC
  * `Autofill` rounds the periodic payment through the same helper when it estimates the `LoanPay` fee
* **`LoanPayments.LatePaymentDue` and `LoanPayments.FullPaymentDue`** (#221, part of #211) compute what a late payment and an early full payment cost at a given parent close time, as rippled's `computeLatePayment` and `computeFullPayment` do, and return a `LoanPaymentDue`: `Principal`, `InterestToVault`, `PaidToBroker` and `Total`. Each returns null where the node refuses that payment: not yet late (`tecTOO_SOON`), already late (`tecEXPIRED`), or one payment left (`tecKILLED`)
  * the lending formulas moved from `LoanSchedule` into the internal `LendingMath`, which both use
  * `LedgerRules.FixCleanup3_4_0` selects whether a payment exactly at `NextPaymentDueDate` is late; `FromNodeAsync` reads it
  * `TestLatePaymentDue_MatchesWhatTheNodeCharges`, `TestFullPaymentDue_MatchesWhatTheNodeCharges` and `TestFullPaymentDue_Iou_MatchesWhatTheNodeCharges` pay a loan late or close it early and compare the node's split with the computed one
* **`LoanOrigination.Compute`** (#222, part of #211) computes the `Loan` a `LoanSet` would create, offline, from the transaction, the vault and the broker, as rippled's `LoanSet` and `computeLoanProperties` do: `PeriodicPayment`, `TotalValueOutstanding`, `ManagementFeeOutstanding`, `LoanScale` and the copied terms, in a `LoanOriginationTerms`. It repeats the checks that depend on the amounts and reports the node's result in `Refusal` and `RefusalReason`: fewer assets available than requested (`tecINSUFFICIENT_FUNDS`), a value the asset cannot hold, a value finer than the loan's scale, and the amortization guards (`tecPRECISION_LOSS`)
  * the internal `AssetRounding` gains `STAmount`'s conversion, exponent, `scale()` and `isRounded`
  * the internal `LendingMath` gains `PeriodicPayment` (`loanPeriodicPayment`); `PaymentFactor` takes the rate, the payment count, `fixCleanup3_2_0` and the context instead of `LoanTerms`; `LoanTerms.Create` builds terms from their parts
  * `LendingProtocol-Guide` (both languages) shows `LoanOrigination.Compute`
  * `TestLoanOrigination_MatchesWhatTheNodeWouldCreate` compares eleven XRP and USD terms with `PreviewLoanSet`: seven created loans field by field, with the schedules projected from each, and four refusals by result
* **`LoanPayments.PaymentForAmount`** computes what a regular `LoanPay` does with its `Amount`, as rippled's `makeRegularPayment` does: as many regular payments as the amount covers, up to 100, then, with `tfLoanOverpayment` on a loan created with it, the rest as an overpayment (`computeOverpaymentComponents`, `tryOverpayment`, `doOverpayment`) that repays principal and re-amortizes the loan. It returns a `LoanAmountPayment`: the split in `Due` (`LoanPaymentDue`), `PaymentsMade`, `IsOverpaid`, the `Loan` as the payment leaves it in `LoanAfter`, or the node's result in `Refusal` and `RefusalReason` (`temBAD_AMOUNT`, `tecNO_PERMISSION`, `tecKILLED`, `tecEXPIRED`, `tecINSUFFICIENT_PAYMENT`)
  * `LedgerRules.FixCleanup3_1_3` selects whether the amount is truncated to the loan's scale before the overpayment is taken from it; `FromNodeAsync` reads it
  * `computeLoanProperties` and `checkLoanGuards` moved from `LoanOrigination` into the internal `LendingMath` (`ComputeLoanProperties`, `LoanGuards`, `LoanProperties`), which the overpayment reuses; `LendingMath.Overpay` ports the overpayment
  * `LendingProtocol-Guide` (both languages) shows `LoanPayments.PaymentForAmount`
  * `LendingMath.LoanGuards` returns the node's result with the reason: a zero-rate loan that comes out with interest is `tecINTERNAL`, as in `checkLoanGuards`, and `LoanOrigination.Compute` reports it instead of `tecPRECISION_LOSS`
  * `TestPaymentForAmount_MatchesWhatTheNodeTakes` pays an XRP loan three payments and a half without the flag and two and a half with it, and previews two refusals; `TestPaymentForAmount_Iou_Overpayment_MatchesWhatTheNodeTakes` overpays a USD loan; both compare the split, the payment count and the loan left with the node's
* **`VaultShares`** (#223, part of #211) computes vault deposits and withdrawals offline from the `Vault` entry and the share count, as rippled's `VaultDeposit`, `VaultWithdraw` and `VaultHelpers.cpp` do: `Deposit`, `WithdrawAssets` and `RedeemShares` return a `VaultQuote` with the `Shares` minted or burned and the `Assets` moved, or the node's result in `Refusal` and `RefusalReason` (`tecPRECISION_LOSS`, `tecINSUFFICIENT_FUNDS`, `tecLIMIT_EXCEEDED`, and `tecPATH_DRY` where the conversion overflows, a vault with assets and no shares outstanding included)
  * the conversions follow `assetsToSharesDeposit`, `sharesToAssetsDeposit`, `assetsToSharesWithdraw` and `sharesToAssetsWithdraw`, with `roundToVaultScale`, `clampToAssetsTotalScale`, the dust checks, the sole shareholder's waived unrealized loss and the final withdrawal of all available assets
  * `LedgerRules` (`Xrpl/Sugar/LedgerRules.cs`) carries the amendment flags for both the lending and the vault helpers
  * `Vault-Guide` (both languages) shows `VaultShares`
  * `TestUVaultShares` pins the empty vault's first deposit and the `tecPATH_DRY` of redeeming shares when none are outstanding
  * `TestIVaultShares` quotes every step of XRP, USD and MPT vaults offline, compares it with `PreviewVaultDeposit` or `PreviewVaultWithdraw` and submits it: deposits, withdrawals by assets and by shares, final withdrawals, and refusals for dust and for more than the vault or the holder has
  * `TestVaultShares_WithUnrealizedLoss_MatchWhatTheNodeMoves` impairs a loan so the vault carries `LossUnrealized`, then compares withdrawals by assets and by shares in the redemption phase with the node; `TestILoanBase.BuildBrokerVaultAsync` takes the investment window
  * `Deposit`, `WithdrawAssets` and `RedeemShares` take an optional `parentCloseTime` and, under `LendingProtocolV1_1`, repeat `getVaultPhase` for a closed-ended vault: a deposit outside the subscription phase is `tecEXPIRED`, a withdrawal in the investment phase `tecTOO_SOON`; `LedgerRules.LendingProtocolV1_1` selects it and `FromNodeAsync` reads it. `TestUVaultShares` pins the phase boundaries; `TestIVaultShares` quotes every step at the validated close time and compares the investment-phase refusals with the node; `Vault-Guide` (both languages) describes the check
* **`AmmLiquidity`** (#224, part of #211) computes AMM deposits and withdrawals exactly, as rippled's `AMMDeposit` and `AMMWithdraw` do under `fixAMMv1_3`, from an `AmmPool` read with `AmmPool.FromAmmInfo`. An `AmmQuote` carries `Asset1`, `Asset2`, `LpTokens` and `PoolAfter` - the balances and LP token balance the ledger will hold - or the node's result in `Refusal` and `RefusalReason` (`tecAMM_INVALID_TOKENS`, `tecAMM_FAILED`, `tecAMM_BALANCE`, `tecPRECISION_LOSS`)
  * deposits: `DepositForTokens` (`tfLPToken`), `DepositBoth` (`tfTwoAsset`), `DepositSingle` (`tfSingleAsset`), `DepositSingleForTokens` (`tfOneAssetLPToken`), `DepositWithEffectivePrice` (`tfLimitLPToken`), `DepositIntoEmptyPool` (`tfTwoAssetIfEmpty`); withdrawals: `WithdrawForTokens` (`tfLPToken`), `WithdrawAll` (`tfWithdrawAll`), `WithdrawBoth` (`tfTwoAsset`), `WithdrawSingle` (`tfSingleAsset`), `WithdrawSingleForTokens` (`tfOneAssetLPToken`), `WithdrawAllOfAsset` (`tfOneAssetWithdrawAll`), `WithdrawWithEffectivePrice` (`tfLimitLPToken`, its `EPrice` in LP tokens per unit of the asset); `InitialLpTokens` gives the LP tokens `AMMCreate` issues
  * `AmmPool.FromAmmInfo` takes the account's trading fee as `getTradingFee` does: the auction slot's discounted fee for its holder and authorized accounts until it expires
  * the internal `AmmFormulas` ports equations 3 to 8, `adjustLPTokens`, `adjustAssetInByTokens`, `adjustAssetOutByTokens` and `checkAMMPrecisionLoss`; the internal `AmountMath` ports `STAmount`'s `divide`, `multiply`, addition and `toSTAmount`
  * `LedgerRules` gains `LargeNumbers`, `FixAMMv1_1`, `FixAMMv1_3` and `MPTokensV2`
  * `AmmMath` points to `AmmLiquidity` for exact figures
  * `TestIAmmLiquidity` runs a token/XRP pool through every covered mode, the discounted fee, the only liquidity provider, the effective-price modes - a deposit taken whole, one capped by the price, one refused - and the withdrawal that deletes the pool: each step is quoted, compared with `PreviewAMMDeposit` or `PreviewAMMWithdraw` for acceptance or the refusal, submitted, and the pool is compared with `amm_info` to the last digit; `TestInitialLpTokens_MatchWhatAMMCreateIssues` compares `InitialLpTokens` and `DepositIntoEmptyPool` with the LP tokens of a new pool
  * `DepositWithEffectivePrice` and `WithdrawWithEffectivePrice` refuse an `EPrice` that is not positive and a negative `Amount` with `temBAD_AMOUNT`, as `invalidAMMAmount` does in preflight; `TestUAmmLiquidity` pins it
* **`BalanceChanges.GetBalanceChanges` reports MPT balances.** It reports a holder's change in `MPTAmount` and, like a trust line issuer, the issuer's side as the opposite of the change in `OutstandingAmount`, as a `Currency` with `MPTokenIssuanceID`. A holder whose modified `MPToken` lists no previous `MPTAmount` is resolved against the change in the issuance's `OutstandingAmount`, taken as zero when the issuance node is absent. `TestUBalanceChangesMpt` covers a vault deposit and withdrawal recorded from a node, and both outcomes of the ambiguous case
* `Tests/Xrpl.Tests/Fixtures/Simulate` holds the recorded `simulate` responses, and its README says where each one comes from
* `LendingProtocol-Guide` and `Vault-Guide` (both languages) show the payment cap and the previews
* `RawJson` and `XrplResponse<T>.Raw` document the cost of a large response (#213): why the frame is allocated per message and cannot be pooled, and that a large `result` read with `Utf8JsonReader` over `RawJson.Span` avoids the second message-sized copy a `JsonElement` result makes
* **`XrplNumber` arithmetic, bit-exact with rippled's `Number`** (#218, part of #211). `Add`, `Subtract`, `Multiply`, `Divide`, `Power`, `Root2`, `Truncate`, `ToInt64` and `Abs` round as rippled's `Number.cpp` does under a `NumberContext`; `+`, `-`, `*`, `/` and unary `-` use `NumberContext.Default`
  * `NumberContext` names the mantissa scale (`NumberMantissaScale`: `Small`, `LargeLegacy`, `Large320`, `Large330`) and the rounding mode (`NumberRounding`: `ToNearest`, `TowardsZero`, `Downward`, `Upward`); `Default` is `Large330` rounding to nearest, `ForAmendments` maps SingleAssetVault / LendingProtocol, `fixCleanup3_2_0` and `fixCleanup3_3_0` to the scale, `WithRounding` changes the mode
  * the engine is `NumberCore` (internal), a statement-for-statement port of the parts of `Number.cpp` that rippled's transaction processing calls, with 128-bit intermediates
  * `Root2` throws `ArithmeticException` on an input where rippled's Newton-Raphson loop would cycle with a period longer than two and never return
  * `TestUNumberVectors` replays 15,935 results printed by rippled's `Number.cpp` at 00606bec1 across all scales, rounding modes and operations; `Tests/Xrpl.BinaryCodec.Test/Fixtures/Number` holds the vectors, the generator and how to rebuild them. `TestUXrplNumberArithmetic` covers the public API
  * `Xrpl.BinaryCodec` exposes its internals to `Xrpl.BinaryCodec.Test`
* Twelve `Xrpl.BinaryCodec.Test` classes take the `TestU` prefix that CI's unit-test filter matches (#218): `TestUBinarySerializer`, `TestUEnumParity`, `TestUFieldDispatch`, `TestUAmount`, `TestUInt32Type`, `TestUInt64Type`, `TestUIouValueTrailingDot`, `TestUIssueMpt`, `TestULoanSetEncode`, `TestUNumberType`, `TestUXChainBridgeType` and `TestUXrplNumber`
* **Breaking: `AMMBid.AuthAccounts` changes from `List<AuthAccount>` to `List<AuthAccountWrapper>`** (#219). Entries in `AMMBid`, `IAMMBid` and `AMMBidResponse` serialize as `{"AuthAccount": {"Account": ...}}`
  * migration: `new AuthAccount { Account = address }` becomes `new AuthAccountWrapper(address)`; read the address as `entry.AuthAccount.Account`
  * `TestUAMMBidAuthAccounts` signs an `AMMBid` with an authorized account and decodes the blob, and reads both shapes; `TestAMMBid_AuthAccounts_TradeAtTheDiscountedFee` submits the bid, finds the account in `amm_info` and compares its deposit at the discounted fee with the node
* **`XrplErrorCodes` covers rippled 3.4.0's error table** (#231): 27 constants added, each mapped by `XrplErrorClassifier`:
  * `slowDown` (429) and `json_rpc` are `TemporaryServerProblem` and retryable; `highFee` is retryable and user-fixable
  * `unlBlocked`, `wrongNetwork`, `internal` and `dbDeserialization` are `ServerState`, not retryable
  * `forbidden` is `UnsupportedRequest`; `delegateActNotFound` and `noPathRequest` are `NotFound`; `masterDisabled`, `badCredentials`, `badSecret`, `badSeed`, `badKeyType`, `badIssuer`, `badFeature`, `channelMalformed`, `channelAmtMalformed`, `domainMalformed` and `oracleMalformed` are `InvalidInput`; `alreadyMultisig`, `alreadySingleSig`, `signingMalformed`, `transactionSigned`, `badSyntax` and `malformedStream` are `BadRequest`
  * an error response without `status` is rejected with `RippledException`, carrying the `ErrorResponse`, instead of a plain `XrplException`
  * `TestUXrplErrorCodes` checks every code of rippled 3.4.0's table has a constant, that no constant classifies as `Unknown`, and the status-less path
* **Breaking: `VaultCreate` no longer declares an `Amount` field** (#208). The property is removed from `IVaultCreate`, `VaultCreate` and `VaultCreateResponse`
  * migration: create the vault without an amount and fund it with `VaultDeposit`
  * `TestUTransactionModelFieldsConformance` checks every wire property a transaction request or response model declares against the fields the vendored `transactions.macro` gives its type. `VaultCreate.Amount` was the only violation
* **Lending rates are documented in their actual unit, 1/10th of a basis point, where 100000 = 100%** (#209)
  * `LoanSet`: `InterestRate`, `LateInterestRate`, `CloseInterestRate`, `OverpaymentInterestRate` and `OverpaymentFee` said 1/100th of a basis point. `LoanBrokerSet`: `CoverRateMinimum` and `CoverRateLiquidation` said the same
  * `LOLoanBroker.ManagementFeeRate` gave its range as 0-100000; it is 0-10000, up to 10%. `LOLoanBroker.CoverRateLiquidation` now states its unit
  * every rate comment on `LoanSet`, `LoanBrokerSet`, `LOLoan` and `LOLoanBroker` carries a worked example (`100000 = 100%, 5000 = 5%`)
  * `LendingProtocol-Guide` (both languages): the broker example labelled `15000` as 150%, `12000` as 120% and `100` as 1%. They are 15%, 12% and 0.1%. The step that presented the rates as an update to an existing broker now sets them on the creating `LoanBrokerSet`, since rippled refuses them on a modification with `temINVALID`. The LoanBroker field table listed `Asset`, `Asset2`, `AssetsAvailable` and `AssetsTotal`, which the object does not have, and now matches `ledger_entries.macro`
  * `LendingProtocol-Guide` (both languages) now creates a closed-ended vault in its walkthrough, which `LoanBrokerSet` requires under `LendingProtocolV1_1` (`tecNO_PERMISSION` on an open-ended one). It states which phase admits the vault deposit (subscription) and the loan (investment), and lists `tecTOO_SOON`, `tecEXPIRED` and the new `tecNO_PERMISSION` causes under Common Errors
  * `TestILoan.TestLoanSet_InterestRateIsInTenthBasisPoints` creates a loan at `InterestRate = 50000` and checks that the node charges 50% a year on it
* **`Hashes.HashOfferId` returns the index the node assigns to an `Offer`**: the namespace is hashed as rippled's `uint16` (`00 6F`), not three bytes. `HashOfferId(string, uint)` added; the `int` overload delegates to it
* **`Hashes.HashTrustline` returns the index the node assigns to a `RippleState`**
  * a 3-character currency code is encoded as the 20-byte code with the ISO letters at bytes 12-14
  * the two AccountIDs are ordered as unsigned bytes, as rippled does, instead of through `BigInteger.Parse`
  * `XRP`, as the ISO code or the all-zero hex code, throws `ArgumentException`
* `TestUHashes` covers every ledger-object helper - `HashAccountRoot` (re-enabled), `HashOfferId`, `HashTrustline` with ISO and hex codes, `HashSignerListId`, `HashEscrow` - with the xrpl.js vectors and two node-assigned offer indices. `TestILedgerObjectIds` creates an account, offer, trust line, escrow and signer list on the stand and checks each helper against the index `account_info` / `account_objects` report

## 11.8.0.0 23/09/2026

* **A Delegate permission reads as its name everywhere, the way rippled reports it** (#197). `Xrpl.BinaryCodec` now understands the PermissionValue field in both of the forms rippled accepts, so a transaction taken from a node, encoded and decoded again comes back as it arrived instead of turning into a number partway through.
  * `DelegatablePermissions` maps a permission between name and value in one place, covering both kinds: granular permissions and transaction types, whose permission value is the type code plus one. It is the counterpart of xrpl.js's `delegatablePermissions` lookup
  * `StObject` reads a name, a number or a number written as a string for that field, and emits the name when it resolves one - matching rippled's `STUInt32::getJson`. Other UInt32 fields are untouched; the mapping is bound to PermissionValue alone
  * `PermissionValueConverter` writes the name as well, and no longer keeps its own copy of the tables. **Signing is unchanged**: the codec encodes either form to the same bytes, which `TestKnownPermissionsStillEncodeForSigning` pins against a fixed blob
  * a value with no name in this version is still written and reported as the number, and a name this version cannot map is still refused by the codec rather than signed as something else
* **Breaking:** `GranularPermission` moved from `Xrpl.Models` to `Xrpl.BinaryCodec.Enums`. It has to live in the codec, because the codec is what names a permission when it decodes one and the dependency runs one way: `Xrpl` sees `Xrpl.BinaryCodec`, never the reverse. Keeping it above would have meant a second copy of the table in the codec. The type shipped for the first time in 11.7.2.0, hours before this release; a consumer that already named it updates the `using`.

## 11.7.2.0 23/09/2026

* **`GranularPermission` gives the twelve granular permissions a type** (#203). They existed in exactly one place - a private `Dictionary<string, uint>` inside `PermissionValueConverter` - so granting one meant writing the magic number `65537` or the bare string `"TrustlineAuthorize"`, neither checked by the compiler, and the set could not be enumerated at all. `PermissionValue = (uint)GranularPermission.TrustlineAuthorize` now type-checks.
  * the converter builds its lookup from the enum instead of restating the table, so the two cannot drift apart
  * they were missed because `definitions.json` does not carry granular permissions and never will - it holds fields, ledger entry types, transaction types and results - so they fell outside the `GenerateEnums` pipeline that produced every other protocol enumeration. Their only source is `permissions.macro`
  * that macro is now vendored into `Tests/Xrpl.Tests/Fixtures/`, sha-pinned in its `.ref` to the same develop commit as `transactions.macro`, and `TestUGranularPermissionConformance` compares the enum against it by name and by value. It also checks that every permission names a transaction type `transactions.macro` declares, and that each value stays above `ushort.MaxValue`, which is what keeps a granular value from colliding with a transaction-type permission

## 11.7.1.0 22/09/2026

* **`DelegateSet` validation matches rippled's preflight, and stops refusing a transaction the ledger accepts** (#199). The local checks existed to catch what the node would reject; three of them did not correspond to anything the node does.
  * **An empty `Permissions` array is accepted.** It is how a delegation is revoked - rippled's `doApply` deletes the `Delegate` ledger object when the array carries no entries - and `ValidateDelegateSet` threw on it, so the only way to withdraw a grant was unreachable through this SDK
  * **`Authorize` must differ from `Account`**, which rippled's preflight rejects with `temMALFORMED` and this validator did not check at all
  * **Duplicate detection compares the resolved name, not the spelling as written.** rippled deduplicates on the numeric `sfPermissionValue`, so `Payment` and `1` are the same entry twice and used to pass. The name is the canonical form rather than the number, because two values this version cannot name have to stay distinct - collapsing them onto one sentinel would invent a duplicate the node does not see
  * **The non-delegable list covers all 26 types rippled refuses to delegate**, not four, and it now sees through the numeric form. It never fired on the typed path: `PermissionEntry.PermissionValue` is a `uint` and the check compared the serialized text against names, so `PermissionValue = 4` (`AccountSet`) passed. The value is resolved to a name through `PermissionValueConverter.TryGetPermissionName` first. Of the 22 names added, 17 are Loan, Vault, Sponsorship and ConfidentialMPT types the protocol gained after the original four were written
    * checking this locally is sound because `TxSettings.delegable` is static: an amendment can withhold delegability from a delegable type - `Permission::isDelegable` takes the network's `Rules` - but never grant it to a forbidden one, so a listed type is refused on every network. The converse set, the 56 delegable types, could not be checked this way
    * a granular permission resolves to its own name and is therefore never matched, which is correct: rippled allows `AccountDomainSet` even though `AccountSet` itself is not delegable
    * `TestUDelegateSet.TestDenyListMatchesRippledMacro` compares the list with the vendored `transactions.macro` - the fixture `TestUTxFormatConformance` already reads - so re-pinning the fixture without extending the list turns the suite red by name
  * the structural checks that do mirror preflight stay: at most 10 entries, no duplicate `PermissionValue`, `Permissions` present and an array of well-formed entries
  * `TestUDelegateSet` covers all of it; there were no unit tests for this validator before

## 11.7.0.0 22/09/2026

* **A Delegate permission name this version cannot map no longer costs the caller the object that carries it** (#195). `PermissionValueConverter` threw a `JsonException` on an unrecognized `PermissionValue` name, and rippled returns that field as a name and gains new ones with every amendment, so a node ahead of the release took the whole containing object with it: through `TransactionResponseConverter` the exception was swallowed and the transaction came back empty with `TransactionType` left at the enum default `AccountSet`, and through `LOConverter` it escaped and failed the entire `account_objects` page.
  * **Breaking:** the converter is now `JsonConverter<PermissionEntry>` rather than `JsonConverter<uint>` and is applied to `PermissionEntry` itself, not to its `PermissionValue` property - a converter bound to the number could not reach a second property. Code that named `PermissionValueConverter` in its own `JsonSerializerOptions` has to drop it; `PermissionEntry` carries the attribute
  * `PermissionEntry.PermissionValueName` carries the name: the token the node sent, or the name resolved from the number when the node sent one. `PermissionValue` is `PermissionValueConverter.UnknownPermissionValue` (0, which rippled treats as no permission at all) when the name does not map
  * `PermissionValueConverter.TryGetPermissionValue` and `TryGetPermissionName` expose both directions of the mapping
  * writing is unchanged for a permission that maps - the number is what `Xrpl.BinaryCodec` reads - and an unmapped name is written back as the name, which rippled accepts in `tx_json` and the codec refuses to sign
  * `TestUPermissionValueConverter` covers the mapping in both directions, the two paths that used to lose the object, and the signing blob

* **`TransactionType.HookSet` is removed** (`Xrpl.BinaryCodec` 11.7.0.0). It was written by hand in the 2022 refactor, outlived the move to generated enums as an `[Obsolete]` leftover, and belongs to no protocol definition: rippled defines no transaction type 22, and neither the bundled `definitions.json` nor the one in xrpl.js has ever carried it. Its ordinal made delegate permission value 23 reachable from the name, a value no ledger can produce.
  * **Breaking:** the field is gone from `Xrpl.BinaryCodec`, so `TransactionType.Values` no longer contains it. A `DelegateSet` naming `HookSet` now fails closed - the name maps to nothing, `PermissionValue` stays 0 and the codec refuses to sign it

* **A transaction whose deserialization fails keeps the type its discriminator named** (#196). `TransactionResponseConverter.Read` catches `JsonException` and returns the bare object built from the discriminator, which it then dropped: no `*Response` class assigns `TransactionType` in a constructor, so the field stayed at the enum default `AccountSet` - a real and common type, not a sentinel - and a `Payment` or a `DelegateSet` that failed to parse presented itself as an empty `AccountSet` with no error of any kind. Only `TransactionResponseUnknown` stamped the field.
  * the catch branch now resolves the discriminator through `ParseTransactionType`, falling back to `TransactionType.Unknown`. The round trip back to a name is compared, because `Enum.TryParse` also accepts the decimal form of a member's value and would otherwise turn a discriminator like `"5"` into whichever member holds that number
  * `TestUTransactionResponseConverter` gains the failed-parse cases: the named type, an unknown type, and the numeric discriminator
  * the fallback object still carries none of the data the failed pass would have assigned - the type is honest, the fields are not recovered

## 11.6.1.0 22/09/2026

* **Per-type interfaces for the five `ConfidentialMPT` transactions** (#191). `IConfidentialMPTConvert`, `IConfidentialMPTConvertBack`, `IConfidentialMPTSend`, `IConfidentialMPTClawback` and `IConfidentialMPTMergeInbox` carry the fields of their type, and the request and the response class of each pair implement one - the pattern every other transaction type follows. Until now the 32 fields of the set were declared twice, once on each half, with no contract between them, so `summary.Transaction is IConfidentialMPTSend` could not be written.
  * `IConfidentialMPTSend` implements `IDestination` as well, as `IPayment` and `IEscrowCreate` do
  * `TestURequestAndResponseHalvesShareAnInterface` no longer excludes these five, so every request/response pair in the assembly shares an interface
  * the README and the remarks on `TransactionSummary.Transaction` drop the exception they documented

## 11.6.0.0 21/09/2026

* **The protocol schema follows rippled develop at `e3c8996e`, the 3.4.0-rc1 build the nightly stand is pinned to** (#182 bumps the pin). `definitions.json` had been synced for 3.3.0 and was eight fields behind develop, which definitions-watch had reported as node-only for three weeks. The nightly-pin bump that would have listed them opened with an empty "definitions.json vs the new build" section: the step inherits `bash -e` from the runner, and the diff exits 1 whenever it finds drift, so errexit ended the step at the assignment - before the report was echoed or recorded - in the one case the step exists for. Fixed alongside.
  * **closed-ended vaults** (rippled #7921, LendingProtocolV1_1): `VaultCreate` and `LOVault` carry `VaultKind`, `SubscriptionDate` and `RedemptionDate`, and the `VaultKind` enum names the two kinds. `ValidateVaultCreate` pins rippled's preflight: the dates only on a closed-ended vault, both of them, with the redemption at least three minutes and less than thirty years after the subscription (rippled #8151 raised the floor from one minute; caught by running the closed-ended flow on the nightly stand). Deposits are accepted in the subscription phase only, withdrawals in every phase but investment
  * **confidential MPT key rotation** (rippled #7915, ConfidentialMPTKeyRotation): `LOMPTokenIssuance` carries `IssuerKeyEpoch` and `AuditorKeyEpoch`, incremented each time `MPTokenIssuanceSet` replaces the key. The transaction is unchanged - the same `IssuerEncryptionKey`/`AuditorEncryptionKey` fields rotate a key once the amendment is active, and the current key is refused with `tecDUPLICATE`. `LOMPToken` carries the holder side, `IssuerKeyMirrorEpoch` and `AuditorKeyMirrorEpoch`: the epoch each mirrored encrypted balance was produced under, which rippled compares against the issuance's epoch to decide whether the mirror is still current, so a rotation marks it stale. `ContractResult` (rippled #7988) is known to the codec but belongs to no format yet
  * **the vendored `ledger_entries.macro` moves to develop `f6b51f0b`**, the commit that placed the mirror epochs on `MPToken`. `TestULedgerEntryFieldsConformance` reads that file in both directions, so moving the pin also named the two Smart Escrow entries it carries, and the models follow them: `LOEscrow` gains `Bytecode` and `Data`, `LOFeeSettings` the voted `GasLimit`, `BytecodeSizeLimit` and `GasPrice`. Ledger-object fields only - the `SmartEscrow` amendment is `Supported::No` on every build, so nothing returns them yet and the transaction side stays out of this release
  * `VaultWithdraw` and `LoanBrokerCoverWithdraw` accept `CredentialIDs`, for a `Destination` that requires deposit authorization; validated the way `Payment.CredentialIDs` is
  * a protocol field is a member on the transaction's interface as well as on its classes, so `IVaultCreate`, `IVaultWithdraw` and `ILoanBrokerCoverWithdraw` each gained one. Anything outside the SDK that implements one of those interfaces - an adapter, a test double - stops compiling until it declares the new member. No default bodies: on a data contract a default would have to accept a value and drop it, which is the silent outcome these interfaces exist to avoid, and unlike `IXrplClient` nothing implements them to add behaviour
  * the vendored `transactions.macro` is pinned to the same develop commit as `ledger_entries.macro` instead of the 3.3.0 tag, so both conformance tests describe the build the nightly stand runs. Up to 3.3.0 the tag and develop agreed on transaction fields; they no longer do
  * `Xrpl.BinaryCodec` 11.6.0.0 for the new codec entries, numbered with `Xrpl` since both move in this release. The CI stand (3.3.0) knows none of the new fields, so the unit suite covers them with round trips and validation, and `TestIClosedEndedVault` drives them against the nightly stand (LendingProtocolV1_1 at genesis, the pin from #182): a closed-ended vault through its three phases, an open-ended one carrying no `VaultKind` on the ledger, and a `VaultWithdraw` to a deposit-authorized destination that is `tecNO_PERMISSION` without `CredentialIDs` and succeeds with them. Key rotation has no stand: ConfidentialMPTKeyRotation is Supported::No, and the shared amendment generator cannot preset a name the 3.3.0 binary does not know
  * the Loan integration suite follows the amendment too. Under LendingProtocolV1_1 `LoanBrokerSet` refuses an open-ended vault ("LoanBroker requires a closed-ended Vault", `tecNO_PERMISSION`) and every Loan test built its broker on one, so all 18 of `TestILoan` failed on the nightly stand - identically on untouched `dev`, which is what said it was the node's rule rather than a regression. `TestILoanBase` now creates a closed-ended vault where the node asks for one and waits for the investment phase before handing the broker back: rippled originates a loan only there, while the vault deposit that funds it is taken only in the subscription phase before it, and the two dates are measured from the ledger's close time rather than the machine's clock, which on a standalone stand is a different clock. A node without the amendment does not know the fields at all - it answers `invalidTransaction`, not a result code - so the open-ended path stays for it, chosen through `AmendmentGuard`
  * what that unblocks, and what it does not: on the nightly stand `TestILoan` goes from 0 of 18 to 7 of 18, and all 11 that still failed reported `Counterparty: Invalid signature` - the role signing prefixes `fixCleanup3_4_0` introduces, which is what the entry above this one goes on to implement, and which was the whole of what remained. `TestISponsoredVaultLoan` is blocked by the same thing on 3.4.x, at `Sponsor: Invalid signature`. Neither failure was about vaults any more, which is what this entry set out to establish; the signatures are the subject of the entry above, and are green there. The CI stand (3.3.0) is untouched by any of it; what the suite looks like there once the signing change lands is counted in the entry above
  * `ValidatedCloseTimeAsync` and `WaitForCloseTimeAsync` moved to `IntegrationTestConfig`: three test classes now need the ledger clock, and each had been carrying its own copy

* **A sponsor's and a counterparty's signature cover bytes of their own** (rippled `fixCleanup3_4_0`, **breaking against nodes without the amendment**).
